### T4a, fix round 2, 2026-10-23: the kerb skirt on the WHOLE garden outline (branch `tb-t4a`; CPU only, no GPU; the see-through is now measured with rays)

**Commits** (every message ends with the Co-Authored-By line): the skirt + its tests (`ponds.js planQuay`, `data/ponds.js QUAY`, `landmarksTauride.js`, `pond-test.mjs`), and the docs commit (this entry, `docs/AREAS.md` 8.12, `tools/tauride/README.md`). The reviewers' findings, each checked first:

| finding | verdict | what was done |
|---|---|---|
| **MAJOR (lens)** the `heightAt` start-up cost still fails the +30 % gate (only the reporting was fixed) | **right; not a code defect; a decision for the lead, nothing changed** | `heightat-bench --repeat 25`, run alone on the final tree: cold median full **31.45 ms** against the Paradny-only **18.84 ms** = **+66.9 %** (+12.6 ms once; warm 4.35 against 2.71 ms = +60 %), `pass: false`; the same machine gives T3 (clean `feat/areas`) +25 .. +27 %. The lens measured 34.35 / 20.71 = +66 % on its machine, the documents say +64 .. +66 %: all four agree. The cause is as written in fix round 1 (the terrain patch covers 97 % of the garden box; a call into `gardenHeight` costs ~150 ns in the cold interpreter) and a cheaper fast path was tried and gained 3 % (reverted). **Decision for the lead: accept +12.6 ms once at start-up (the build of the page takes seconds), or re-base the gate on T3 + 30 % (about 33 ms: it would pass). The phase is NOT gate-clean on this point and this entry does not call it so.** |
| **MAJOR (qa)** a pale-blue sliver (the sky through a hole) still runs beside the far path at T05 (~25 m), along the dam channel's east bank, at the NE end of the quay and at the canal mouth | **real, measured** | The first quay (fix round 1) stood only where the WATER outline lies within 10 cm of the garden outline (170 m). `gardenHeight` at the outline (walked every 10 cm): the floor is not at the street level (y 0) on **255 m of the 2,153 m** of outline: **219.7 m below it** (down to -0.45: the 170 m of the quay and 49 m of banks that have only partly risen where the water stands 1 .. 3 m off the outline: the QA's four runs at (1251,-1714)..(1263,-1700) 23 cm, (1274,-1684)..(1281,-1663) 27 cm, (1282.7,-1643)..(1283.1,-1639) 41 cm, (1435,-1590)..(1437,-1590) 35 cm) and **35.6 m above it** (the crest, +0.10, reaches the outline: the same slit seen from the street, 5 .. 10 cm tall; nobody had reported it). Outside the outline `heightAt` is exactly 0 all round (4,071 probes 0.3 m out, 0 non-zero), the environment ground is the plane y 0 with its hole inset 6 cm. Fixed: the skirt below. |
| **BLOCKER (qa-gate)** quay: no see-through step at the lake's north shore | **real, the same defect** | the same fix; the gate is now a ray test (below). |

**What was done**
1. **The kerb skirt** (`ponds.js planQuay(floor, ring, Q, lift)`, the table `QUAY` of `data/ponds.js`): a vertical wall ON the garden outline, **stitched to the floor plan itself**: the open edges of the plan (`planTerrain`, cover mode) that lie within 2 mm of the outline (found through a 4 m cell index of the 66 outline edges: 12 .. 18 ms for the 22.6 k triangle plan) are walled from the height of the edge (+ the 5 mm lift of the floor mesh) to the street level (`top` 0): a floor BELOW the street gets a wall that faces the garden (the viewer on the water and the banks), a floor ABOVE it one that faces the street (the viewer on the pavement); steps of 2 cm or less (`drop`) get none; an edge that crosses the street level is cut there (two halves, opposite sides); consecutive edges of one outline edge within 3 mm of their chord (`merge`) become one quad (the quay along the lake is flat: one quad per outline edge); the wall reaches 3 cm (`under`) past the surface it meets (below the floor edge, below the street) so no crack opens. **124 triangles in 62 quads over 265.5 m of the outline (223.5 m below the street, 42.0 m above it), the 0.45 m of the quay the highest step**; one draw (the one of fix round 1, `quayMesh`, granite grey, one-sided); the pond service: 2 draws, 325 triangles. The names `quay`, `planQuay`, `QUAY`, `quayMesh`, `quayTriangles`, `quayLength` of the service stay. `planPonds({ floor })` takes the floor plan (`landmarksTauride.js` passes `tplan`); without it there is no skirt (the pure tests of the water and the colliders do not plan a floor).
2. **Why stitched to the plan and not to `gardenHeight`**: the vertices of the floor plan on the outline are the exact heights of `gardenHeight` (`planTerrain` evaluates it at every vertex, the cut points of the clip included) and the mesh is linear between them, so a wall that uses those vertices follows the mesh edge exactly: no gap and no poke whatever the 6 cm tolerance of the floor. A wall built from samples of `gardenHeight` would have stood up to 6 cm off the edge (a 6 cm slit or a 6 cm curb). The old constants (`bottom` -0.7, `tol` 10 cm, `minRun` 0.5) are gone.
3. **Numbers**: the gate of fix round 1 holds: floor 22,609 + water 201 + skirt 124 = **22,934 triangles, +13,854 over the T3 floor (<= 15,000)**, +2 draws (<= 6); the module is **658,899 triangles** (fix round 1 658,797; T3 642,069; BUDGET 700 k = 94 %), 72 meshes, 1,465 colliders, build 973 ms (995 before: no change).

**Tests** (`tools/tauride/pond-test.mjs`: **61 checks, 55 with `--no-env`**; the five quay checks of fix round 1 are replaced by seven):
* the skirt is ONE never-batched mesh of 124 triangles, one more draw, grey, planned in < 250 ms (16 ms);
* **every stretch of the outline where the floor is more than 2 cm off the street has a wall that spans the step**: the outline walked every 5 cm, 5,058 samples (217 m below, 36 m above) with the mesh height 2 mm inside, each covered by the union of the wall triangles to 5 mm: **0 uncovered**; and **the four runs the QA measured** (363 / 435 / 126 / 72 samples): 0 uncovered;
* every corner of the wall lies on the outline (1 mm) and is at the street level, 3 cm under it, or on a vertex of the floor plan (+ lift, or 3 cm under it): 0 at another height;
* the walls face the right way: 76 triangles over a floor below the street face the garden, 40 over a floor above it face the street (0.5 m in front is inside / outside, 0.5 m behind the other), 0 wrong windings;
* **the ray test** (the see-through as the eye has it): the geometry the page draws near the outline (the floor mesh + lift, the skirt, the water plane inside the union, the environment ground = the plane y 0 outside the hole of `HOOKS.groundHoles`; front faces only, every material is one-sided), **17,185 rays** from viewers on the water side (1.5 / 5 / 12 m inside the outline, eyes 0.2 .. 1.7 m) and on the street side (2 / 6 / 15 m outside) through the gap of the outline (every 0.5 m, five heights between the floor edge and the street): **0 see the sky** (`--fine`: every 5 cm, 169,900 rays, 0; 2.3 s). Mutant without the skirt: 4,211 of 4,510 rays (93 %) go through. The ray test found one defect of the first draft of this fix (28 of 17,185 rays through a 3.5 cm gap at a zero crossing of the step, where an edge with its ends on both sides of the street level got one wall that faced the wrong way: the edge is now cut at the crossing);
* mutants: an outline moved 500 m, a step threshold of 1 km and no floor plan give no wall; a merge tolerance of 0 gives 465 quads instead of 62 (the merge works); a merge tolerance of 1 km (chords that leave the floor edge) leaves 772 samples uncovered.

**Decisions and why**
* **The lip (floor above the street, 42 m) gets a wall too**, facing the street: the same slit, seen from the pavement at a grazing angle (a ray through the 5 .. 10 cm between the street and the floor edge goes under the floor and sees the sky). It is one sign in the same code; without it the ray test fails for the street side. It reads as a kerb of up to 10 cm: a GPU look.
* **No `gardenHeight` samples, no water-outline logic**: the quay along the lake is the special case of the rule (a floor at -0.45 on the outline), so there is one rule and not two.
* **The wall top is the street level 0 exactly** (the environment ground is the plane y 0; it overhangs the wall by 6 cm, `GROUND_HOLE_INSET`, so no gap shows above it).
* **The skirt is in the outline pass** (not `noOutline`), like the floor: a GPU look (the toon line on its top edge).

**Checks run** (cwd = the worktree, `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8000`; logs in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T4a/fix2/logs/`, the oracle lanes in `.../fix2/take`): `pond-test` OK (61; `--fine` OK), `ground-test` OK (85), `tauride-height-test` default OK and the real build OK, `north-hooks-test` OK (204), `north-keepout-test --parallel --footprints` OK (18), `north-height-test` 324 OK, `north-people-hooks-test` OK (10) and `--scene` OK (20) (these two are run plain, not through `area.mjs`), `hooks-syntax-check` OK (20 files), `tauride-cams-check --colliders` OK, `tauride/data-test --no-regen` OK (138), `paradny-walk` OK, `relief-check`, `wildlife-test` OK, `people-test --at tauride` with `pond-people-probe` OK (0 walkers in the water, 0 nav nodes in the water) and with `people-probe` (the hills) OK; `take.sh strip-light north-light checks` against `m6-baselines` (`compare.mjs --only strip-,chk-`): **the strip is unchanged**: `layout-golden --diff` identical (strip and North), `npm run check` identical in all 25 modules, the streets / city / environment signatures identical (+0), garage OK; `areas-test`, `import-lint`, `runtime-only-lint`, `gen-area-html --check`, `layout-test`, `landmarks-groups-check`, `views-check`, `owns-table --check` OK (`chk-hooks` is the baseline's old FAIL, now OK 204). `npm run check:north`: landmarksTauride 658,899 triangles; the other modules identical. `tools/golden/layout-north.json` did NOT change (`heightAt` and `groundAt` are the terrain function, the skirt is not). Every `src/` edit changes the bake revision: re-bake at the merge.

**Open for the GPU QA and the lead**
* **For the lead:** the `heightAt` gate (above): accept +12.6 ms once or re-base on T3 + 30 %.
* **GPU QA:** the skirt from the lake side at the quay, at T05, at the dam channel's east bank, at the NE end of the quay and at the canal mouth (colour #8b8a84, the toon outline on its top edge, no hairline against the street ground; the wall is 2 .. 45 cm tall); the lip walls on the street side (up to 10 cm: do they read as a kerb or as an edge defect; if they disturb, `QUAY.drop` 0.05 drops the 2 .. 5 cm ones, or a darker colour); the 6 cm hole inset of the ground (the ground overhangs the wall's top by 6 cm).

### T4b, 2026-10-23: the bridges, the dam, the reeds, the duck lanes, the pedestrians on the decks (branch `tb-t4b` from `feat/areas` 3d999e8; CPU only, no GPU frame)

**Commits** (every message ends with the Co-Authored-By line): the kit (`data/bridge-design.js`, `bridges.js`, `reeds.js`, the entry, `ponds.js` deck cuts + look, `paths-draped.js`, `environment/water.js` LOOK), the engine hooks (`people/nav.js`, `wildlife/ducks.js`, `lib/area-tables.js`, `hooks.js`, `hooks/water.js`), the tests (`tools/tauride/bridge-test.mjs` new; `tauride-height-test.mjs` section BRIDGES + `lib/height-harness.mjs deckRing`; `pond-test`, `ground-test`, `pond-people-probe`, `north-hooks-test`, `north-keepout-test`, `heightat-bench`), and the docs commit (this entry, the T4a / T4b row of section 3, section 10, `docs/AREAS.md` 8.13 and 9, `tools/tauride/README.md`).

**The lead's decisions, recorded as stated** (the T4b brief): the `heightAt` start-up cost of T4a is ACCEPTED as built (+12.6 ms once: the cold pass over the garden lattice 31.45 ms against the Paradny-only 18.84 ms); **the gate from T4b on: the cold pass must not grow by more than +10 % over T4a (<= 34.6 ms), and `deckY` must not enter `layout.heightAt`** (the bridges are a deck SERVICE, not terrain: `heightat-bench --repeat 25` prints the verdict as `fullVsT4a`); the 15 k floor gate of T4a held as floor + water + skirt (+13.9 k); the bank-zone ribbon pass of `paths-draped.js` is T4b's (done below: back under the T3 budget of 12,000); the kerb skirt lip walls stay unless the GPU QA says otherwise; the two engine files of the landing hook (`core/player.js`, `main.js`) are accepted.

**What was done**
1. **The bridge kit** (`landmarksTauride/bridges.js`, the design values in `data/bridge-design.js`, the mapped facts in `data/bridges.js`): three types and the weir on the six crossing sites, built as ONE merged mesh with vertex colours (toon, one draw) + the dam's water decal (one draw). **The axis** of a site is the data's deck polyline EXTENDED at both ends until the end stands on dry land past the shore colliders of T4a (the -0.3 contour + 0.6 m: five of the twelve data ends stood ON the contour) or to the foot of the stair that the data draws at the end (the wooden bridge: w415779498 / w420650661). **`deckY(x, z)` is the WALKING PROFILE along the whole axis** (the approach ramp or stair from the alley, the deck, the far approach), sampled into a table of knots at most 0.25 m apart with the kinks of the profile on knots: a bbox reject, a projection onto the axis segments (half width 1.5 + pad 0.45) and one linear interpolation; pure, no allocation, the same bits on every call, 2.8 ms for the 80,868-point lattice of the garden box (the bbox rejects almost everything). The head of every deck is the ribbon surface of the alley (the floor mesh + 0.03) exactly: no step, no gap. The bridge table as built:

| site | type | ways | axis (extended) | heads | deck | over the water | triangles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---:|
| No.1 | humped wooden | w112853204 | 19.74 m from the foot of w415779498 (1098.7,-1594.1) to the foot of w420650661 (1109.3,-1577.5): the two stairs (1.71 / 2.78 m) + the 15.3 m deck | 0.67 / 0.69 (0.6 above the stair feet, the T3 rule) | hump +0.45: crown 1.13 | wet 6.6 .. 16.2 m | 1,572 |
| No.2 | flat metal | w112853220 | 14.13 m (B end +0.75 m) | ramps 2.0 / 2.6 m (<= 1:4.5) | flat 0.41 | 4.5 .. 12.7 | 972 |
| dam | weir | w112880645 | 11.13 m (+0.5 / +0.75 m) | ramps 0.65 / 0.5 m | the walkway on the crest at 0.0 (the street level at its east end, where the garden outline runs 0.3 m east of the way end) | 1.4 .. 9.6 | 820 |
| No.3 | iron arch (the Big Bridge) | w81524417 + w882132271 + w882132272 | 21.80 m (the chain, no extension) | ramps 5.6 / 3.5 m (1:8.6 / 1:5.4) to the abutments at the shore, level 0.73 | arch +0.35: crown 1.07, 5 ribs | 6.0 .. 17.9 | 2,172 |
| No.4 | iron arch (the Small Bridge) | w882128034 | 16.43 m (no extension) | ramps 1.9 / 1.8 m to the abutments, level 0.34 | arch +0.40: crown 0.74, 4 ribs | 2.3 .. 14.2 | 1,628 |
| No.5 | flat metal | w112880644 | 16.09 m (A end +0.25 m) | ramps 2.3 / 2.0 m | flat 0.41 | 1.4 .. 13.2 | 1,100 |

Deck width 3 m (`BRIDGE_DECK_WIDTH`, a DESIGN value), slopes outside the stairs at most 0.214 per metre (`MAX_RAMP_SLOPE` 0.22; the plan's walking rule 1:4), every deck at least 0.3 m above the water where it crosses it. Granite only where real: the abutments at the shore (iron, metal, the dam), small footings under the wooden heads; marble terminal posts at the iron and metal abutments; cast-iron balustrade (posts, top and mid rail, an X lattice per bay) on the iron arches, plain rails elsewhere, wooden rails and two beams under the planks (planks by colour, 0.5 m) on No.1; iron arch ribs from the abutment faces at the water up to the deck underside at the crown (5 on No.3, 4 on No.4). **The player walks the same profile**: 92 sticky walk ramps (`physics.addWalkRamp`, pieces of at most 1 m between the knots, never across a kink or a polyline vertex: the ramp IS the table, within 0.2 mm on the arch) and 28 rail boxes (0.5 m thick from the deck edge outward, their top 1.3 m above the deck under them: a hop (apex 0.73) + STEP_HEIGHT (0.45) cannot clear them). The ramps run 0.15 m under the rails on each side: the first version left a 5 cm slit between the ramp edge and the rail, and the deck ring (below) found a walker who dropped through it from the wooden stair onto the bank below the deck.
2. **The shore ring opens onto the decks only** (`ponds.js planPonds({ decks })`, `chordOutside`): the T4a chords are CUT where a deck rectangle (the axis segments, half width + 0.45 m) crosses them, the pieces outside stay (15 chords cut, 48.4 m; 195 -> 204 boxes); the 0.3 m gap extension of a kept piece reaches under the rail, so the ring and the rails close without a wedge. The T4a `gaps` hook stays for the tests.
3. **The stairs of the alleys at the wooden bridge** (`paths-draped.js`): a stair whose end lies at a bridge way end rises from the ground at its foot to the DECK HEAD (`ground.deckAt`, the kit's profile), whatever the terrain does under it (w420650661 stands on a plinth over the bank: 4 x 0.139 from 0.08 to 0.64; w415779498 4 x 0.147 from 0.07 to 0.66); `PATHS_DRAPED.bridgeRise` is gone (the one number is `bridge-design.js TYPES['wood-humped'].headRise` 0.6, the T3 rule "4 steps = 0.6 m"). The bridges are planned BEFORE the alleys for this; the ground of the bridge heads is the floor mesh (`meshHeight ?? gardenHeight`), so a head meets the ribbon surface bit for bit.
4. **The dam**: ONE water level (`WATER_Y` -0.45, the T4a decision: the union is one polygon, the colliders, the mask and the bowls are built on one level; the OSM data gives no second level and a visible step would need a second water mesh and a split union). The walkway (w112880645, 9.9 m, extended to 11.13 m) lies on the crest of a solid stone body across the channel at 0.0 = the street level at its east end (`DAM.crest`; `QUAY.top` is the same 0: the dam's east abutment is the street kerb); the overflow is a VISUAL: a granite ledge 0.12 m above the water on the north face (fix round 2: that is the SMALL POND face, the lake is south: `DAM.spillToLake`), a decal sheet of falling water from the ledge to the water and a foam strip 0.9 m wide on the water at its foot (4 transparent triangles, out of the outline pass). Granite abutments at both banks, iron rails. **Fix round 1 (the GPU QA: a flat grey slab, no stone visible):** the granite BODY rises to the crest (the slab sits in it, 0.45 m of stone above the water on both faces, 0.25 m beyond the deck on the lake / channel (south) face), a COPING 0.15 m ABOVE the deck line on the spill face (the Small Pond, north), the nappe hangs from its lip to the water (two rows bulging 0.16 m, one translucent tone with a brighter thread per metre), the foam's far edge is wavy; warm light granite against a dark walkway (`DAM.bodyOut`, `copingRise`, `copingW`, `sheet`, `foam`; 90 decal triangles).
5. **The deck service and the pedestrians**: `ctx.services.landmarksTauride.deckY = courts.deckY ?? bridges.deckY` (bit for bit `paradnyDeckY` on the podium, the profile on all eight bridge ways, null elsewhere); **`deckLift(x, z)`** = `WALK_LIFT` 0.02 on a garden bridge (the deck is the walking surface), null on the podium (the engine's pavement lift 0.12 stays); **`walkways`** = the densified axis of every deck. `people/nav.js` (three strip-neutral hooks, all behind `HOOKS.deckServices`, which the strip does not name): a point on a service deck is NOT water (`water()`: the garden bridges cross the ponds, which are in the field's water mask), `surfaceY` adds the service's `deckLift` instead of 0.12 where it answers, and the services' `walkways` become footway nodes and edges like `streets.paths` (every 3 m, `edgeOK`): **the mapped bridge ways are not in `streets.paths` at all** (`streets/paths.js` skips `r.bridge`), so without it no edge of the graph crosses the water: the probe of the nav graph showed the two end nodes of every bridge in the main component and NO edge between them, and 0 walkers on any deck in 5 minutes. With it: 42 nav nodes on the decks, all at `deckY + 0.02` (5 cm), the gate and the island's alleys in one component, 235 walker samples on the decks in 5 minutes (feet at deckY + 0.02: p50 0.03 m, p90 0.07, worst 0.25 in motion on the hump: the `groundY` easing of `people/sim.js`, as on the hills), 0 walkers and 0 nodes in the water off a deck.
6. **The reeds** (`reeds.js`, `REEDS` of `bridge-design.js`): one `InstancedMesh` of 500 clumps (7 blade triangles each, vertex colours base -> tip with dry tips, an instance tint; 3,500 triangles, one draw, out of the outline pass, LOD 700 m), placed by walking the four shore rings by arc length with a slow belt noise (period 23 m, threshold 0.64: 133 belts over 806 m of the 2,666 m shore, 42 % of the 10 m stretches empty), a clump every 0.9 m (jittered) at a shore distance drawn from -0.55 .. +0.35 m (the shallows and the wet foot), its feet on the terrain; none within 3.5 m of an alley end, 2.2 m of an alley, 4 m of a stair, 3 m of a deck (the extended rectangle), 5 m of the dam, 3 m of the garden outline (the quay and the skirt stretches), none outside the garden; the Small Pond's centre is empty by construction. 765 candidates thinned evenly to the 500 of the plan. Deterministic (a hash of the ring and the step).
7. **The duck lanes** (`HOOKS.duckPonds`, `hooks/water.js makeDuckPonds`, the 14th key, validated in `lib/area-tables.js`; `wildlife/ducks.js` pond branch): the hook gives the water level (`WATER_Y`), the four shore rings with the water on the LEFT of travel (the ducks' frame of the bank chains), the offset (4.2 m) and the margin (3 m), the six deck keep-outs (centre, half length + half width + 3 m) and the exact shore distance; `ducks.js` samples the rings like the bank chains and keeps a lane when every sample is water for the field (`env.waterAt`, the lane code's own assertion) AND at least 3 m from every shore (the hook's distance) with 2.5 m of free water beyond and no deck near; lane key `'pond'`, `lane.y` the pond level (the waterway lanes carry no `y` and keep their expression). The North: 15 pond lanes (296 samples, 0 dry, 0 near a shore, 0 at a deck) + the 3 Fontanka lanes of its far south-west corner that the old branch always made; 5 groups, 19 ducks, all on the ponds, on the water over 2 simulated minutes; `wildlife-test` North lane RESULT OK (the test already allowed the `pond` key), the strip lane RESULT OK.
8. **The T4a leftovers**: (a) the narrow arms (the GPU QA's pale speckled gravel / cracked ice from 50 .. 250 m): `environment/water.js` LOOK `'tauride-pond'` darker and more teal (deep #1e3a33, mid #2b4e42, shallow #5c7246, chop 0.9 instead of 1.5) and `ponds.js pondMaterial` patches the shader with `data/ponds.js POND_LOOK`: the shallow tint fades out over 2.4 m from the bank instead of 5 m (an 8 .. 12 m arm was olive from both sides), the ripple iso-lines x 0.4 and the sun glints x 0.3 (the speckles), the bridge decks as deck zones of the water (the soffit shadow, kept to the deck + 1.5 m and a factor 0.45 instead of the near-black of a street bridge); **the GPU QA re-judges from the aerials** (its round 1: water from the N, S and W aerials, from T12 and at eye level; still a pale speckled band from the EAST at 110 .. 250 m with the sun ahead: fix round 1 below). (b) The mutants no test caught: the ring without the deck cuts, the service without the bridges, a low rail, no ramps, a 0.4 m wooden head, no reeds, no keep-outs (all in `bridge-test`), the MODULES gating of `duckPonds` (`north-hooks-test`); fix round 1: the nav water exemption of the decks (`pond-people-probe` pins the crossing: the lens found it unpinned); `QUAY.under` and the garden branch of `nav.surfaceY` are still unpinned (open below). (c) `docs/AREAS.md` 9.1 `HOOKS.waterBodies`: `env.shoreDist` does NOT know the ponds (it is `layout.shoreDist`, the waterways; `env.waterAt` / `env.kindAt` read `field.shore`); the stale `tools/tauride/water-mask-check.mjs` reference and the absolute `D:/` path in the `pond-test` header are gone. (d) `paths-draped.js`: a row is five wide only where IT needs it (T3 made a whole way wide when one of its rows did: 30 of 106 ways, 8,560 of 13,768 triangles), a row beside a curved one is wide too (so the zip of a 3- and a 5-row happens where the terrain is flat across), two rows of different width are zipped, and a triangle that would fold over is dropped (2, the T4a slivers): **11,084 ribbon + 524 stair triangles = 11,608 <= the T3 budget of 12,000** (T4a 13,768 + 484), `foldedDropped` and `wideRows` in the stats; the chord rule (1.2 cm) is unchanged. The faceted shore silhouettes are the data polygon's: left.

* Tests: `bridge-test --ducks` **47 checks** (mutants in every group), `tauride-height-test` real build RESULT OK (RELIEF, PONDS, BRIDGES), `pond-test` 61 OK, `ground-test` 85 OK, `north-hooks-test` 206 OK (fourteen keys), `north-keepout-test --parallel --footprints` 18 OK, `north-height-test` 324 OK, `north-people-hooks-test` OK (and `--scene`), `hooks-syntax-check` OK (20 files), `tauride-cams-check --colliders` OK, `tauride/data-test --no-regen` OK, `paradny-walk` OK, `relief-check` OK, `pond-people-probe` RESULT OK, `people-probe` (the hills) OK, `people-test --at tauride` OK, `wildlife-test` strip and North OK, `drive-edge-test` 960 runs, 1,556 hits, 0 stuck.
* **`heightat-bench --repeat 25`, run alone on the final tree** (the T4b gate): the cold median over the garden lattice **34.10 ms against the T4a measure 31.45 ms = +8.4 %, `fullVsT4a.pass: true`** (the gate +10 % = 34.6 ms). Honestly read: `layout.heightAt`, `gardenHeight` and the patch list are byte-identical to T4a (`deckY` is a service), and the same run's Paradny-only baseline is 21.06 ms against T4a's 18.84 (the machine, not the code: the T4a ratio +66.9 % is +61.9 % today); warm 4.44 ms (T4a 4.35).
* **Strip unchanged** (`take.sh strip-light north-light checks` against `m6-baselines`, `compare.mjs --only strip-,chk-`; logs in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T4b/take`): `layout-golden --diff` identical (strip AND North: `tools/golden/layout-north.json` did not change), `npm run check` identical in all 25 strip modules, the streets / city / environment signatures identical (+0), garage strip and North OK, every `chk-*` gate the same; the strip's field raster the same bytes as the clean tree (sha1 6435bf0e..., `field-dump`), the North field raster the same bytes as T4a's (sha1 d2f0f182...: nothing of the field changed); `drive-edge-test` 960 runs, 1,556 hits, 0 stuck; `wildlife-test` strip OK (the strip's lanes are the waterway lanes as before). Against the T4a take (`compare.mjs <T4a fix2 take> <T4b take> --only north-,chk-`): the North golden and streets signature identical, `north-check` differs in `landmarksTauride` only as built (**668,023 triangles** = T4a's 658,899 - 2,644 ribbons + 8,268 bridges + 3,500 reeds; 75 meshes (+3: the bridges, the dam water, the reeds), 734 instances (+500 reeds), 1,594 colliders (+129: 9 of the ring cuts, 120 of the bridges), BUDGET 700 k = 95 %), in `wildlife` (+4,592 triangles, +40 instances: the pond ducks) and in `people` (+1 instanced mesh: the walkers' graph has the decks now; a random-stream cascade of the kind T1 / T2 recorded); the other twelve modules identical.

**Decisions and why**
* **`deckY` is the walking profile, not the structure**: the service answers what a foot stands on (the approach ramp, the stair, the deck), so the walker, the car handling and the harness have one function and no special case at a head; `nav.surfaceY` lifts it by 0.02 (`deckLift`), not by the podium's 0.12, because a 3 m footbridge shows a floating walker where a 74,000 m2 podium does not.
* **The ring is cut, not gapped**: the T4a `gaps` hook removed whole chords within a radius (a 14 m chord cut at a 2 m radius would have opened 12 m of shore); cutting the chord at the deck rectangle opens exactly the deck between its rails.
* **The ramps run under the rails** (widthInset -0.15): the one hole the deck ring found.
* **ONE water level at the dam** (above): the honest reading of the data; a step would be a second datum.
* **`walkways` in `nav.js`** goes beyond the brief's "ONLY: the water exemption and surfaceY": without it the pedestrians cannot use the bridges at all (the bridge ways are not footways for `streets`); it is three lines behind the same `deckServices` table and the strip's list is empty. Reported in the problems for the lead.
* **The reeds stand on `gardenHeight`**, not on the floor mesh (the mesh's hidden-bed tolerance is up to 25 cm under the water): their feet are under the surface where it matters and the test checks the function.
* **The `fontanka` lanes of the North** (3, at x -400) are the old waterway branch (`allBanks()` keys) and were there before T4b: left as they are.

**Commands that verify it** (cwd = the worktree; `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8000`): section 10 (the T4b block) and the T4a block; `node tools/area.mjs north tools/north-hooks-test.mjs`, `... north-keepout-test.mjs --parallel --footprints`, `... north-height-test.mjs`, `node tools/north-people-hooks-test.mjs [--scene]`, `node tools/area.mjs north tools/wildlife-test.mjs`; the strip oracles as T4a (`take.sh strip-light north-light checks` + `compare.mjs`, `field-dump`, `drive-edge-test`, `wildlife-test`).

**Open for the GPU QA, T5 / T6 and the lead**
* **GPU QA**: the five bridges and the dam from the heads, the middle of the deck and the water side (T02, T04, T05, T06; do the iron arches with granite abutments, the humped wooden bridge with its stairs on the bank, the flat metal footbridges read as the real ones in the toon style, or as grey planks; the dam's ledge, sheet and foam at T05; z-fighting of the dam body against the water; fix round 1: the granite body, the coping and the nappe from the spill face (NORTH, the Small Pond side: the `dam2-northface-*` poses, yaw about 180), the lake / channel face (SOUTH, the `dam2-lakeface-*` poses, yaw 0: the plain body, no sill there by design: fix round 2) and the walkway); the reed belts at 5 .. 30 m (density, repetition of the 7-blade clump, the tint); the ducks on the ponds over 40 s; the narrow arms from 50 .. 250 m (is it water now: the look tune of `POND_LOOK` is the knob); the soffit shadow under the decks; the walk south gate -> memorial with the player; pedestrians crossing the bridges; frame cost at the bridge poses.
* **T5 / T6**: the reeds and the bridges claim nothing (the trees of T5 must keep off the decks and the reed belts: `svc.bridges.keepOut`, `svc.reeds.list`); the fence of T6 along the north shore and the gates; the rails are invisible colliders 1.3 m above the deck: a bench or a lamp on a deck is not planned.
* **For the lead**: the `walkways` hook in `nav.js` (above); the memorial loop of the island (10 nav nodes round the stele, reached only through the steps ways that `streets.paths` skips) is a component of its own since T3, not touched here; the mutants still unpinned: `QUAY.under` (the 3 cm overlap of the skirt), the garden branch of `nav.surfaceY` (a walker on a bank: `pond-people-probe` sees 6 samples in 5 minutes, too few to pin a deletion); the flight landing on a deck over the water is refused as 'water' (`noLanding` reads the terrain under the deck): acceptable, noted.

### T4b, fix round 1, 2026-10-23: the crossing pinned, the dam as a granite weir, the pond's sun terms from the air (branch `tb-t4b`; CPU only, no GPU; software-GL stand-in views of the dam and the east aerials)

**The reviewers' findings and what was done** (verified first; the three were real):
1. **Lens (major): the nav water exemption of the decks was not pinned.** Confirmed: `people/nav.js water = rawWater && !westDeck && !areaDeck` with `&& !areaDeck(x, z)` deleted left `pond-people-probe` green (26 walker samples "on a deck", all on the land approaches: the walkway nodes are added without a water test, only the EDGES over the water fail `edgeOK`). The probe now asserts the crossing itself: (8) for every bridge site `nav.route(nearestNode(axis end A), nearestNode(axis end B))` is non-null and the route has a node over the water (exact shore distance < 0) on a deck; (9) walker samples on a deck OVER the water > 0. The shipped tree: `6 sites, 0 without a route` (No.1 6 nodes / 2 wet, No.2 6 / 2, dam 4 / 1, No.3 6 / 2, No.4 5 / 3, No.5 5 / 2), 235 deck samples of which **144 over the water**, RESULT OK; **the mutant** (the term removed, `--seconds 40`): `6 without a route: No.1 NO ROUTE ... No.5 NO ROUTE`, `26 samples, 0 of them OVER the water`, **RESULT: FAIL** (logs in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T4b/fix1/probe-*.log`). `nav.js` itself is untouched.
2. **QA (major): the dam read as a flat grey footbridge slab, no granite mass, the sheet a flat pale plank, the foam a pale rectangle.** Confirmed from the shots: the body stopped at the slab underside (0.21 m above the water), all three greys the same. Now (`data/bridge-design.js DAM`, the weir branch of `bridges.js buildSiteSolids`): the granite BODY rises from under the bed to 1 cm under the crest (the walkway slab sits in it: 0.45 m of stone above the water on both faces) and runs `bodyOut` 0.25 m beyond the deck on the lake / channel (south) face; a granite COPING `copingW` 0.8 m wide stands `copingRise` 0.15 m ABOVE the deck line on the north face (the sill, seen from the deck and from the Small Pond; fix round 2: this entry first called it 'the lake (north) face', which is wrong: the lake is south); the nappe hangs from the coping lip to the water in two rows bulging 0.16 m out from the face, one translucent pale tone (the granite shows through) with a brighter 0.12 m thread per metre; the foam strip's far edge is a wavy polyline (the width at every metre mark from a hash). Colours: body / abutments a warm light granite `#a39b8e`, the coping `#b7b0a3`, the walkway a dark paving `#6b6a66` (the rails as before). The first cut of this round had the nappe in two tones in regular 0.35 m stripes and the foam in stepped columns: on software GL a striped curtain and a sawtooth, replaced before the commit. ONE water level stays (the T4a datum). The kit is 8,354 triangles (8,264 solids + 90 decal; was 8,268), the colliders unchanged (120: the coping and the body are inside the rail boxes' reach, nothing new to walk on), the deck profile unchanged (`tools/golden/layout-north.json` identical; `deckY` is the table). `bridge-test` section 6 asserts it: the weir level = the crest, 4 .. 400 decal triangles, no decal vertex under the water or above the lip, decal vertices ABOVE the deck line (the nappe hangs from the coping), solid vertices at the lip (18) and at the body top (18) inside the dam's box.
3. **QA (major): the narrow canal round the pitch island still a pale speckled band from the EAST aerials at 110 .. 250 m** (water from the N, S, W aerials and at eye level). Confirmed in the shader: with the sun ahead the engine's low-sun GLITTER SHEET (`sheet * 0.26`, a warm white ADDED over all the sun-facing water: the pale base of the band) was untouched by the T4b tune, and the glints (the speckles) were only x 0.3. `data/ponds.js POND_LOOK` gains `sunSheet` 0.5 (the factor on the glitter sheet) and `air` [0.3, 0.55] (the view elevation, the up component of the direction to the camera, over which BOTH sun terms fade out: gone when the camera looks down at more than about 30 degrees; at eye level the sun's path on the water stays); `ponds.js pondMaterial` patches the two lines (`uPondB` is a Vector4 now, `uPondC` the air range); `environment/water.js` is untouched (the LOOK entry stays as T4b). Software GL (`bridge-preview.mjs --old`, the east aerial at 110 m, yaw 100): the old sun terms give the pale speckled band on the west canal, the fixed ones dark water; the same pair at the eye-level poses is the same picture.

**The software-GL stand-in** (`tools/tauride/bridge-preview.mjs` + `.html`, the pattern of `ground-preview`): builds the floor, the ground material, the bridges and the dam, the alleys, the ponds with the patched water shader and the reeds in the module's order (no environment: the water has no field), renders the QA's dam poses and the east aerials on SwiftShader; **8 programs compiled, 0 errors** (the patched pond shader among them); `--old` is the before / after pair of the sun terms. ### T4b, fix round 2, 2026-10-23: the dam's faces come from the data (branch `tb-t4b`; CPU only, no GPU)

**The reviewer's finding and what was done** (verified first; it was real):
1. **Lens (major): the overflow visual (coping + nappe + foam) was built on the NORTH face and documented as "the lake (north) face", but in the data the lake lies SOUTH of the dam.** Confirmed on the plan: the lake mouth `data/bridges.js DAM.mouthOnLake` (1273.8, -1655.1) lies on the +z (south) side of the dam axis (1267.2, -1672.3) -> (1277.6, -1676.4); all 18 lip vertices and all 270 decal vertices lie on the -z (north) side, which is the Small Pond (`data/ponds.js SMALL_POND`: the bed BED_SMALL north of `mouthZ` -1655.3; the dam at z -1674). The round-1 code chose the face by a compass guess (`lakeSide` = the -z side) and the comments, the T4b item 4, the round-1 item 2, the GPU QA list, `docs/AREAS.md` 8.13 and `bridge-design.js` all named it "the lake (north) face". **Decision**: the sill and the nappe STAY on the north face, because that is the downstream face: the water came up the Ligovsky canal into the Big Pond (the lake, upstream) and left through the Small Pond down the Samoroyka to the Neva (north), so the overflow dam of the 1790s spilled from the lake INTO the Small Pond. The choice is now a design value and the geometry follows the data: `bridge-design.js DAM.spillToLake` (false = the sill and the nappe on the face AWAY from the lake, `bodyOut` on the lake face; true flips all three to the lake face), and the weir branch of `bridges.js buildSiteSolids` takes `lakeSide` from the sign of `DAM.mouthOnLake` against the axis perpendicular (no compass). The shipped geometry is IDENTICAL to fix round 1 (18 lip vertices north, 270 decal vertices north; 8,264 + 90 triangles, 120 colliders; the `spillToLake: true` mutant mirrors the lip and the decal to the south face: 18 / 270 south, 0 north). `bridge-test` section 6 now asserts the face from the data inside the dam check (the lake mouth's side of the axis, the lip and every decal vertex on the spill side: "0 lip + 0 decal vertices on the wrong face"; still 47 checks). The docs are corrected in place (the T4b item 4, the round-1 item 2 and its stand-in paragraph, the GPU QA list, section 10's preview command, `docs/AREAS.md` 8.13, `tools/tauride/README.md`), and `bridge-preview.mjs` names its dam views by the face they see: `dam-north-low / -5m / -10m` (the spill face: the sill and the nappe) and `dam-lake-5m` (the lake / channel face to the south: the plain body).

**For the GPU QA** (its round-2 sets `logs/sets/dam2.txt`): the sill, the nappe and the foam are on the NORTH face: the `dam2-northface-low / -eye` poses (yaw about 180, looking south at the dam) and `dam2-channelface-5m` (1272, 0.6, -1679.5, yaw 180) see them; the `dam2-lakeface-5m / -10m` and `dam2-southface-*` poses (south of the dam, yaw about 0) see the plain granite body with `bodyOut` and the dark walkway, with no sill and no sheet BY DESIGN. A re-judgement of "the lake face" must look at the north-face shots.

**Oracles** (cwd = the worktree; logs in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T4b/fix2/`): `bridge-test --ducks` RESULT OK (47 checks), `tauride-height-test --model north --build landmarksTauride --assert-cars --no-selfcheck` RESULT OK (the route arrives over the decks, DECK RING 5,691 runs 0 entered, the mutants red), `npm run check:north` `landmarksTauride` 668,109 triangles, 75 meshes (as fix round 1); **strip unchanged** (`take.sh strip-light north-light checks` against `m6-baselines`, `compare.mjs --only strip-,chk-`: `layout-golden --diff` identical, `npm run check` identical in 25 modules, the streets / city / environment signatures identical, every `chk-*` gate the same; the only CHANGED row is again `chk-hooks` exit 1 -> 0, the foundation baseline's own failing run; the MISSING rows are the heavy lanes not taken). No bake, no GPU.

**Open**: the hydrology behind `spillToLake` false (the lake upstream of the Small Pond) is the fixer's reading of the garden's water system, not a mapped fact: the lead confirms it or flips the one value; the GPU QA's re-judgement of the dam from the north-face poses; the items of the round-1 entry.

### T5, 2026-10-23: the garden's own trees (the border belt, the lime avenues, the groves, the solitary oaks, the shore planting, the larch, the 7 OSM trees), the T4b leftovers (branch `tb-t5` from `feat/areas` 4113f2f; CPU only, no GPU frame)

**Commits** (every message ends with the Co-Authored-By line): `c371ea1` the planting (`vegetation.js`, `data/vegetation.js`, the entry, the ground mask from the crowns, the `treegeo.js` default parameter, `data/ground.js`), `61d579c` the `'garden'` reason of `HOOKS.treeReject` (+ `north-hooks-test`, `north-keepout-test`), `b1de50c` the reeds, `5ecfb1d` the tests and the T4b leftovers (`vegetation-test`, `tree-people-probe`, the pins in `bridge-test` / `pond-test` / `north-people-hooks-test`, the relative `heightat-bench` gate, the collider counts, the T11 pose, the BUDGET tripwire, `.gitignore`), and the docs commit that holds this entry (`docs/AREAS.md` 8.14, 9.1, 9.5, 8.13; `tools/tauride/README.md`).

**The lead's decisions, recorded as stated** (the T5 brief): `DAM.spillToLake = false` is CONFIRMED (the lake is upstream, the dam spills north into the Small Pond, the Samoroyka ran north to the Neva: the T4b fix-round-2 geometry stands). **The user CONFIRMED the hill heights of section 7.1 on 2026-10-23**: the table stands as the design values of the garden (sections 7.1 and 11 say so now; the "table the user confirms" wording is gone). The module's BUDGET tripwire in `tools/check.mjs` is raised 700 k -> **765 k** = the measured 695,273 (T4b 668,023 + the 996 trees at the HERO's LOD3 + their blobs + the 700 reed clumps) + 10 %; Paradny decimation stays a T7 decision.

**What was done**
1. **The planting** (`landmarksTauride/vegetation.js planVegetation`, pure and deterministic: a keyed `mulberry32` per zone, no `ctx.rng`, so the plan is the same bits in node, in the browser and in the tests; the zones, species, keep-outs, clearings and budgets are `data/vegetation.js`, every number a DESIGN value). Zones as built, in the planting order (the packing couples a tree to the ones placed before it: the old trees of the belt take their places first, the avenue limes of the perimeter alley fill in between them):

| zone | rule | candidates -> placed (medium) | species as built |
|---|---|---:|---|
| osm | the 7 mapped OSM trees (`data/furniture.js OSM_TREES`), old oaks of scale 1.2 .. 1.4; T5 forced them through every rule but the water (one stood on the asphalt of the perimeter alley: the QA of fix round 2); since fix round 2 every keep-out applies and a refused node is nudged to the nearest clear spot within `ZONES.osm.nudge` 4 m | 7 -> 6 (4 nudged, 1 refused) | oldOak 6 |
| meadow | solitary old oaks on the mown meadows (`ZONES.meadow.solitary`: the T10 meadow (1162,-1386), (1205,-1340), the lake-west lawn (1288,-1462), the east strip (1455,-1560), (1462,-1470); (1225,-1705) in the north lobe is refused by an alley), each with a 15 m clearing that no other tree enters | 6 -> 5 | oldOak 5 |
| belt | the band 1.5 .. 14 m inside the fence: a 6.5 m lattice jittered 2.6 m, thinning from 9 m inward (a soft edge to the lawns), dissolved 9 m round the 11 gates and the 13 fence openings; the perimeter alley runs through it (the way rule keeps the trunks off: 251 of the 512 candidates) | 512 -> 104 | oak 42, lime 31, larch 10, ash 11, maple 10 (40 / 30 / 10 / 10 / 10 %: the plan's mix) |
| avenue | limes on both sides of the 21 `main` alleys (3.5 .. 4.5 m; `avenue.classes`), the trunks 2.75 m off the ribbon edge (never on the ribbon), 7.5 .. 9.5 m apart, 6 m clear of the alley ends | 1,034 -> 491 | lime 342 / limeB 162 before the clearing of the solitary oaks; 491 kept |
| shore | alder (the ash family small and dark: the engine has no alder), birch and shrubs on the CREST band of the banks (`crestAt` = 2 (BANK_TOP - WATER_Y) x the local slope of `data/ponds.js`, + 0.3 .. 2.6 m inland: never on the slope below the crest), walked along the four shore rings of `reeds.js shoreRings` every 8 .. 12 m; none on the quay / skirt stretches (the outline rule), the reed belts, the heads | 265 -> 60 | alder 25, birch 14 + birchB 6, shrub 15 |
| grove | the 23 OSM wood polygons: a 9 m lattice jittered 4 m, thinned by a slow noise (26 m period, 25 .. 100 % kept: clearings and dense knots, not a plantation), 12 % shrubs | 698 -> 329 (+ 40 shrubs among them) | birch 56 / birchB 31 / maple 68 / lime 69 / ash 55 / oak 19 / elm 13 among the trees (the plan's mix within 2 points) |

The species draw is tilted towards the species that lag behind their share among the trees placed so far in the zone (`pick(R, mix, counts)`: the keep-outs refuse a wide old oak more often than a lime, and the first version drifted to 31 % oak in the belt); the draw stays random and deterministic. **The plan: 996 instances at medium (938 trees + 58 shrubs), 681 at performance** (`BUDGET.tierFactor.low` 0.68: each tree keeps or goes by its own hash, so the performance tier is a SUBSET of the medium one; the OSM trees and the solitary oaks always stay), 996 at high (the plan is the budget). Species as built: lime 429 + limeB 156, oak 59 + oldOak 12, maple 72, ash 63, birch 66 + birchB 35, elm 11, larch 10, alder 25, shrub 58.
2. **The keep-outs** (`KEEP_OUT`; `tools/tauride/vegetation-test.mjs` measures each with its OWN geometry against the data modules, 0 violations, and a mutant per rule plants trees there): the 13 footprints + 3 m (to the trunk surface), every way ribbon (half width + the 0.28 m pad) + 1.5 m to the trunk surface, **no leaf lower than 2.5 m over a ribbon** (`GARDEN_SPECIES.base` = the lowest leaf of the family's LOD0 canopy at scale 1, pinned to the real geometry within 2 %: oak 2.99, lime 4.04, maple 3.32, ash 4.58, elm 5.98, birch 3.82 / 3.71, larch 0.90, shrub 0.13: a larch or a shrub keeps its whole radius off the alleys; 594 crowns reach over a ribbon, 0 below 2.5 m), the water (the exact shore distance > 0) and the bank slope (every zone but the shore keeps 1 m inland of the crest), the extended deck rectangles + 3 m, the 12 axis ends + 5 m, the dam axis + 5 m, the 6 stairs + 4 m, every reed clump + 1 m (+ the trunk radius), the camera poses + 4 m and a look corridor 30 m long, 5 m half width tapering (`CLEARINGS`: the 11 ground poses and the two garden VIEWS, pinned to `tools/tauride-cams.mjs` and the North VIEWS), the pitch / 3 playgrounds / fitness station + 3 m, the fountain 14 m, the three monuments 8 m, the memorial ellipse M + 2 m, the gates and the openings 9 m, the service lane + 1.5 m, the outline 1.5 m, the orangery site (outside the outline), slopes over 0.18 for the old species (0.24 for all: none exist), the packing 0.55 of the summed radii (0.42 with a shrub). Several rules overlap (the decks, heads, stairs and reeds lie on the banks that the bank rule keeps clear; the poses stand on alleys that the way rule keeps clear): their mutants relax the covering rules with them so that the measure of THAT rule is what turns red (the test says so).
3. **The build** (`buildVegetation`): the ENGINE's trees. `treegeo.js canopyGeometry / trunkGeometry` and `leafmat.js canopyMaterial / coniferMaterial / trunkMaterial` (the material cache of `ctx.mat.toon` gives the SAME `env-canopy` / `env-conifer` objects as the environment's trees; the bark painter is copied character for character under the same `env-bark-pb-*` keys, so the texture cache hands the environment's textures over and the module draws no new canvas when the environment built first), one `InstancedMesh` per family x LOD as `environment/trees.js` (10 families: ash, birchA, birchB, elm, larch, limeA, limeB, mapleA, oak, shrub -> 40 canopy + 18 trunk meshes, shadows on LOD 0 / 1, `frustumCulled` false), `tree-instances.js createTreeInstanceWriter(list, shapeOf)` with a `shapeOf` that puts every instance at `y = gardenHeight(x, z)` (the banks and the hills: 33 trees above 0.3 m), the LOD assignment on the camera with the environment's distances 42 / 150 / 360 and its 10 % hysteresis, re-packed when the camera moves 2.5 m (no view cone and no cell grid: the garden is one 580 x 530 m box), the first assignment from the page's HERO (what `check:north` counts). Contact-shadow blobs under the 872 trees on flat ground only (a flat disc on a bank or a hill flank would cut into the slope). **Trunk colliders**: a cylinder per tree (996, all in the play area) from `min(0, y) - 0.5` to `y + 4.5` (1.3 for a shrub): the player on a hill is blocked at every height and `people/nav.js` (cylinders with `y0 < 1.7`) takes every one as an obstacle. The real `Player` walking at a trunk from 3 m stops at it (12 of 12). **The larch** is a garden-local species row (`SPECIES_EXTRA`: `kind: 'tiers', open: true`, H 20 / R 3.4 / trunk 0.45, the lighter larch green of `LEAF.larch`) through the ONE engine edit of T5: `canopyGeometry(fam, lod, F = SPECIES[fam])` and `trunkGeometry(fam, lod, F = SPECIES[fam])` take an optional species row (a default parameter: the strip's calls and bytes of behaviour are unchanged, the strip oracles below are identical; `SPECIES` / `FAMILIES` of the engine are NOT touched, so the strip's `buildTrees` iterates the same families). Its four LODs are 80 / 48 / 32 / 10 triangles. No willow: a weeping form has no engine geometry kind and would need a new layout (not cheap): not done.
4. **The ground follows the planting** (`ground-material.js planGroundMask` takes `canopy: [{ x, z, r }]`, the planted crowns of `plan.canopy`, shrubs excluded): a disc per tree with the weight 0.5 at the crown edge and 1 three metres inside it (the litter of the shader, `LOOK.litA .. litB` 0.62 .. 0.78, starts ~1.5 m inside the crown edge); the OSM woods and the authored belt (`CANOPY_BELT`, width 8 -> 12) stay the FALLBACK of a mask planned without a planting (the T3 tools). Why this way and not the planting following the mask: the woods are 88,356 m2 and a planting dense enough to cover them (1 tree / 60-80 m2 = 1,100-1,500 trees in the groves alone) is above the whole budget; a mask from the crowns is 12 lines and the litter lies under the trees that are there by construction. The entry plans everything first (the floor, the bridges, the alleys, the ponds, the reeds, the trees), then the mask, then the meshes. Measured on the built mask: **100 % of the 32,364 litter cells lie under a crown** (the T3 mask planned from the woods: 36 % of its 83,087), 100 % of the 45,050 canopy cells (R >= 0.5) within 3.5 m of a crown, 100 % of the interior of the big crowns is litter; the litter is **16.3 % of the garden** (the OSM woods were 44.7 %); the mown stripes gain the ground between the crowns (76,570 stripe cells, T3 73,179). The mask is deterministic (the built one equals a fresh plan bit for bit).
5. **The hook**: `HOOKS.treeReject` (hooks/keep-out.js, H03) answers `'garden'` inside the garden outline (`hooks/people.js inGarden`) when `landmarksTauride` is built, after the podium and before the campus / Suvorov. The facade's `TREES` carry no ids to claim, and `plant()` applies this reject to the mapped trees too; the procedural trees were already kept out by the claimed park and woods, so the only change in the environment is the 7 OSM trees (`env.treeStats.rej['osm:garden']` 7; `green:garden` 17 = tries on the lake island's wetland polygon that `free` refused before). `north-hooks-test` 207 checks (+ the garden probe, the oracle = the facade's own ring), `north-keepout-test` 18 (its region oracle learnt the ring too). The strip exports no `HOOKS`.
6. **Wildlife**: the module publishes `services.landmarksTauride.treeList = [{ x, z, y, r, h, tr, sp, shrub? }]` (the shape of `environment.trees`) and `trees.list`; **not a one-line service**: `wildlife/world.js` reads the tree lists of the landmark gardens from a hard-coded list `['landmarksE', 'landmarksF', 'landmarksH', 'landmarksJ']` and its trunk grid from `env.trees` only, so the crows' tree-top perches and the ground birds' trunk avoidance do not see the garden's trees until that line names `landmarksTauride` and the trunk grid takes the list (an engine file outside T5's ownership: reported in the problems; T7 or the lead). The ducks are unaffected. `wildlife-test` North and strip OK (the pond lanes exempted, see 8 (d)).
7. **Pedestrians**: `tools/tauride/tree-people-probe.mjs` (the `--eval` of `people-test`, 4 spots x 60 s): every one of the 996 trunks is an obstacle of the nav (`nav.obstDist` <= 0 at the axis), 0 nav nodes inside a trunk (1 within 2 m), 35,020 walker samples, 106 within 3.5 m of a trunk, **0 inside a trunk**. The walkers keep to the ribbons (the trunks stand 1.5 m + their radius off the ribbon edges, so a walker on an alley is 2 .. 4 m from the nearest trunk surface; the first version measured "within 2 m" and saw nobody).
8. **The T4b leftovers**: (a) the reeds (`reeds.js`, `REEDS`): ONE geometry of three sub-clumps side by side (5 / 4 / 4 blades, height factors 1.0 / 0.78 / 1.18: three shapes under the random yaw), 13 blades, a non-uniform scale per instance (`wide` 0.7 .. 1.3, `tall` 0.75 .. 1.25), two rows (a clump stands in the shallows, -1.1 .. -0.32 m, or on the wet foot, -0.32 .. +0.35, 55 % in the shallows: 385 of 700), denser belts (spacing 0.7, threshold 0.58: 154 belts over 1,022 m of the 2,666 m shore, T4b 133 / 806 m), **700 instances, 9,100 triangles, still 1 draw**; `bridge-test` section 8 (band, belts, keep-outs, determinism) green with the new table; the clump split -0.32 (not -0.3: the datum grep of `bridge-test` forbids the contour literal). (b) `heightat-bench.mjs --repeat 25`: the gate is RELATIVE: `fullVsT4a.ratio` = the cold median of the full list over the Paradny-only one of the SAME run against `ratioMax` = 1.10 x the T4a ratio 31.45 / 18.84 = 1.67 -> **1.84**; the absolute comparison is still printed (`absoluteWouldPass`). (c) the pins: `bridge-test` asserts `HEAD_LIFT` (0.03) === `PATHS_DRAPED.offset` (48 checks); `pond-test` asserts `QUAY.under`: every skirt wall reaches 3 cm past the lower surface it meets (0 of 4,000+ samples short) and a skirt planned with `under: 0` is short on more than half of them (62 checks); `north-people-hooks-test` pins the garden branch of `nav.surfaceY` (the text of the term, and in `--scene` 100 bank points of the garden at the terrain + 0.02 on the real nav). (d) `wildlife-test` EXEMPTS the key `'pond'` from its river check: it proves only that the pond lanes do not break the test; that the lanes and the groups stay on the pond water is proved by `bridge-test --ducks` (one sentence in the T4b item 7 and `docs/AREAS.md` 8.13). (e) **T11** moved from (1456.5,-1578.5) yaw 49.9 (the GPU QA: a yellow building in the frame, the palace front at its edge; the building is `w1274934136`, 10 m north of the outline, 10 m to the right of that pose) to **(1442, -1556) yaw 35.5 pitch 3.4**: on the east bank 7.3 m from the shore, 10 m south of the foot of mound E (the relief rule holds), the whole garden front x 1347 .. 1420 at z -1642 .. -1652 spans 32 degrees round the look axis, the lake in front; `tauride-cams-check` (12 poses + 2 VIEWS, with `--colliders`), `views-check`, `tauride-height-test` (the pose margins) green; the CLEARINGS row and section 8 follow. **Fix round 1:** this was checked on the CPU without the environment's trees; the GPU QA found the facade behind the OSM wood w1274934137 on the palace side (the fix-round-1 entry: the palace parterre clearing). (f) `.playwright-mcp/` in `.gitignore`.

**Decisions and why**
* The canopy of the ground comes from the planting, not the planting from the mask (item 4): the budget, and the QA's measure ("dark litter without a canopy over it") is then true by construction; the T3 fallback path stays for a mask planned alone.
* The belt is a band lattice, not rows along the fence: the perimeter alley (w425201986, w1051226582) runs 3 .. 8 m inside the fence, where rows at fixed offsets died to the way rule (the first version placed 50 of 514); the band fills whatever the alley leaves, and the avenue limes of the perimeter alley are the belt's lime share.
* The belt is planted BEFORE the avenues: with the limes first, the belt's oaks and larches found no room (104 placed against 52).
* The species draw is tilted (item 1): the authored shares hold within 5 points without a quota that would print a regular order.
* No view cone and no cell grid in the LOD assignment (the environment has both for its 6,565 trees over 4 km): the garden is 996 trees in one box; the per-instance cost is the same.
* `treeReject` rather than a claim for the 7 OSM trees: the facade's `TREES` is a flat `[x, z, ...]` without ids; the reject is the engine's own path for mapped trees (the Paradny podium uses it already).
* Shrubs stamp no litter in the mask (r ~1 m); the blobs skip the slopes.
* The pose clearings carry a look corridor (not in the brief: "+ 4 m"): a tree 6 m in front of T11 would hide the palace; the engine clears the same shape round its VIEWS. (Fix round 1: the screen in front of T11 was not the garden's but the environment's wood on the palace side: the parterre clearing.)

**Numbers** (CPU)
* `landmarksTauride` (`npm run check:north`, the take of this entry): **695,273 triangles** (T4b 668,023: + 19,820 trees at LOD3 + 1,744 blobs + 5,600 reeds (700 x 13 - 500 x 7) + 86 of the T4b fix-round-1 dam decal), 134 meshes (+59: 58 tree buckets + the blob mesh), 62 instanced meshes, 2,802 instances, 59 materials (+9: the shared canopy / conifer / 7 bark materials counted for this module too), **2,590 colliders** (+996), 3 update functions (+1: the LOD assignment), canvasPixels 262,144 = T4b (the module drew no new canvas: the bark and blob textures are the environment's cache hits), BUDGET 765 k (the tripwire: the measure + 10 %), build 1.3 .. 2.3 s; the other 14 modules of the North identical to the T4b take.
* The trees: 996 instances (938 + 58 shrubs), 996 colliders, 872 blobs, 58 instanced meshes (+1 blob mesh); triangles of the LOD buckets **19,820 at the HERO** (every tree at LOD3 + its stub), **254,199 in the middle of the garden** (1150,-1420), 200,001 at the south gate, the worst case (every tree at LOD0) 1,773,121 (never: LOD0 reaches 42 m); draws 11 at the HERO, 52 in the garden (buckets with instances + the blobs). The engine's oak LOD0 is 1,756 triangles + the trunk 192: the garden's cost per instance is the environment's. The plan 87 ms, the mask 134 ms, the module build 1.3 s in node.
* The module's colliders 1,594 -> **2,590** (+ 996 trunks); the field rasters are the same bytes as T4b (North sha1 d2f0f182...) and as the clean tree (strip sha1 6435bf0e...): nothing of the field changed.
* `heightat-bench --repeat 25`, run alone on the final tree: cold medians over the garden lattice **old 24.33 ms, Paradny-only 26.91 ms, full 43.70 ms: the ratio full / paradny = 1.624 against the T4a ratio 1.669 (ratioMax 1.836): `fullVsT4a.pass: true`**; warm 2.83 / 3.07 / 5.04 ms. The machine was loaded (the other batches had just finished: the `old` mode, which has no patch at all, reads 24.3 ms against ~21 ms in the T4b take), so the ABSOLUTE comparison that the T4b gate made would have failed (43.7 ms against 34.6 ms, `absoluteWouldPass: false`) although `layout.heightAt`, `gardenHeight` and the patch list are byte-identical to T4b (the trees are a module build, not a height patch; the North golden is identical): the relative gate measures the code, not the machine. **Open for the GPU QA, T6 / T7 and the lead**
* **GPU QA** (the only thing the CPU cannot show): does the garden read as the real one (the dark belt along the fence at T01 / T09 / T10 / the aerials, the lime avenues along the main alleys, the open meadows with their solitary oaks, the groves with clearings, the lighter shore planting at T04 / T06 / T11), or as a plantation (the avenue limes are 7.5 .. 9.5 m apart at one offset: the first thing to judge; `ZONES.avenue.spacing` and the scale ranges are the knobs); the larches (12, in the belt; fix round 1: a narrow cone on a clear stem with its underside, a greyer light green: do they read as larch at 10 .. 40 m now; the first round's open tent read as a paper cut-out at 12 m and as a lime-green spruce at 25 m); the litter under the crowns from the air (100 % by construction: is the 16 % share right, is the 1.5 m inset of the litter inside the crown edge right: `GROUND_TUNING.canopyFeather`); trees floating or sinking on the banks and the hills (33 trees stand above 0.3 m; the blobs are left off the slopes); the LOD popping at 42 / 150 / 360 m; the frame cost at T10 / T12 / T01 (254 k triangles of trees in the middle of the garden at medium: if a frame is worse than 25 ms, the LOD0 distance or the avenue spacing is the knob, not the geometry); the reeds at 5 .. 30 m (two rows, three shapes); the T11 frame (the palace front across the lake).
