### T5, fix round 1, 2026-10-23: the larch redone, the palace parterre cleared for T11, the zone geometry pinned (branch `tb-t5`; CPU only, no GPU; the larch checked as a side-view plot of its geometry)

**The findings** (two reviewers and the QA gate): (1) **lens, major**: no test pinned the zone geometry (a grove planter that ignored the wood polygons passed 72 / 72, the tier cap hid the surplus; a belt widened to 40 m was caught only by a collateral mutant). (2) **QA, major + the gate blocker**: from the T11 pose the palace front was hidden by a row of broad crowns, and the frame with the garden's 58 tree meshes hidden was pixel-identical: the screen was the ENVIRONMENT's planting of the palace side. (3) **QA, major**: the larch did not read as larch: at 12 m three flat tiers on a 0.9 m stick (no underside: a paper tent), at 20-25 m a lime-green stacked-cone spruce.

**What was done**
1. **The palace parterre** (finding 2; `src/areas/north/hooks/keep-out.js`): the facts first (`forecourt-facts`, in the scratchpad of the round): the lake's north shore IS the garden outline (z -1607 .. -1639 between x 1283 and 1418), the palace's garden front stands 20 .. 35 m north of it (the central bow x 1360 .. 1394 at z -1642.6, the wings at -1651 .. -1658), and that strip is the OSM wood **`w1274934137`** (11,641 m2, U-shaped round the palace, outside the park polygon: 155 of the 157 environment trees in x 1280 .. 1470, z -1700 .. -1600 are its), which the engine plants at wood density up to the facade; the T11 sight line leaves the garden at 71.5 m and crosses 32 m of that wood with 6 crowns on it (maple r 4.6 at 2.1 m off the line, birch at 0.1 m, maple r 4.6 at 4.1 m ...). "Move T11 to the quay" cannot work (the wood stands between the quay and the facade). The smallest fix in the phase's files: **`HOOKS.treeCleared` (H02, the engine's own idiom for view corridors) clears `PALACE_PARTERRE = [1356, 1404, -1660, -1606]`** (the lawn in front of the central bow, the bow's width + 4 .. 10 m to each side, from the quay to the facade) when `landmarksTauride` is built; the wood keeps its trees on both sides of the parterre (137 remain in the box above outside it), the mapped trees go too (a soft clearing; no mapped node stands there), the counter `wood:cleared` 28. **The sight line is free (0 crowns within r + 0.5 m of it), the environment plants nothing in the parterre (0 of 20 before).** This is a design decision taken by the fix round (the vista from the lake to the palace's garden front is the historical axis of the ensemble; the alternative, a formal parterre planting by the garden itself, is T6 / T7's): one data row, reversible, for the lead to confirm. The two hook tests learn the region (`north-hooks-test` 208 checks: the parterre probe (1380, -1630) answers `[null, true, false, false]`, `treeCleared` serves `landmarksSmolny` AND `landmarksTauride`; `north-keepout-test` 18: the parterre probe, the region in the lattice sweep, the parterre ring among the records that re-roll in `real` vs `off`).
2. **The larch** (finding 3; `data/vegetation.js SPECIES_EXTRA.larch`, `GARDEN_SPECIES.larch.base`, `LEAF.larch`; `environment/treegeo.js`): the engine's `addTiers` hard-coded the lowest tier at 0.9 m for an open row and left the underside out, so no table row could give a clear stem; `treegeo.js` now reads three OPTIONAL row keys, **`y0`** (the height of the lowest tier; default `open ? 0.9 : 0.55`), **`under`** (the dark underside; default `!open`) and **`stem`** (the tiers trunk `[height, foot radius, top radius]`; default `[2.2, 0.16, 0.07]`), and the LOD2 stub takes `y0` (default 1.0): **every default is the constant it replaces, no engine row has a key, and the geometry of all 16 engine families x 4 canopy LODs x 2 trunk LODs is bit-identical** (sha256 of every attribute and index before and after the edit: `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T5/fix1/treegeo-{before,after}.json`, `cmp` equal; `vegetation-test` pins the spruceA / spruceB hashes and the default expressions by text). The larch row: **H 21 / R 2.4 / y0 3.6 / under / stem [4.3, 0.27, 0.16] / tiers [7, 5, 3, 2]** (was H 20 / R 3.4 / tiers from 0.9 m / no underside): a slender cone on a clear 3.6 m stem whose tiers read as volumes (LOD0 140 triangles = 7 x 10 x 2, LOD1 80, LOD2 26, LOD3 10), `base` 3.6 (the test pins it to the geometry's lowest leaf), the palette a greyer light green (`#6f9a44 #769f4a #68923e #7aa44f`: L 0.41 .. 0.48 above every lime hex, S 0.35 .. 0.40 against 0.44 of the first round's `#7fa842`). Side view against the old row, the spruce and a lime: `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T5/fix1/larch-side.png`. The head-clearance mutant of section 3 lost its low wide species with this (the larch's crown starts at 3.06 m at scale 0.85, a shrub's crown never reaches past the trunk clearance): it now plants a FIXTURE species (a young oak, scale 0.5 .. 0.7, crown base 1.5 .. 2.3 m, r 2.7 .. 3.8 m) as the whole grove mix: with a table that claims base 9 the rule is fooled (low crowns over the ribbons), with the true base it keeps every one of them off (0 low).
3. **The zone geometry** (finding 1; `vegetation-test` group 2b, on the BUILT list): every grove tree inside an OSM wood polygon (334, 0 outside), every belt tree 1.5 .. 14 m inside the outline (118), every avenue tree 2.75 m (+- 0.8) off the ribbon edge of a main alley of 40 m or more (488), every shore tree on the crest band (60), no grove tree on a MEADOWS block outside a wood, and `stats.planned == stats.instances` at medium (1,012 = 1,012 < 1,150: the cap is idle). Mutants: the woods fed as their bounding BOXES (136 grove trees outside, the plan 1,150 = the cap), the belt widened to 40 m (164 off the band), the avenue offset 6 m (405 off).

**Numbers** (CPU): `landmarksTauride` (`npm run check:north`) **695,603 triangles** (T5 695,273: + 16 trees at LOD3 + their blobs; the larch's LOD3 is the same 10 triangles), 134 meshes, 62 instanced meshes, 2,833 instances (+31), **2,606 colliders** (+16), BUDGET 765 k; the planting **1,012 instances at medium (953 trees + 59 shrubs; 12 larches) / 692 at performance**, 887 blobs: the larch's narrower crown (r 2.25 against 3.4) frees room in the belt packing (T5: 996 / 681). The LOD buckets 20,120 triangles at the HERO, 247,781 in the middle of the garden, 208,203 at the south gate, draws 11 / 50. The ground mask: litter 16.6 % of the garden, 100 % under a crown. The environment (`check:north` against the T5 take): 10,468 -> 10,448 instances and 6,049 -> 6,029 colliders (-20: the parterre's trees; `wood:cleared` counts 28 refused candidates, the wood's stream re-rolls), 6,545 trunks in the North, 0 inside the outline, `osm:garden` 7.

**Oracles** (cwd = the worktree, `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8000`; logs in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T5/fix1/logs/`, the take in `.../fix1/take/`): `vegetation-test` **89 checks** OK (72 + 17), `north-hooks-test` **208** OK, `north-keepout-test --parallel --footprints` **18** OK, `check:north` OK, `views-check` OK, `tauride-cams-check --colliders` OK, `bridge-test --ducks` 48 OK, `pond-test` 62 OK, `ground-test` 85 OK, `data-test` OK, `tauride-height-test --model north --build landmarksTauride --assert-cars --no-selfcheck` OK, `north-height-test` OK, `north-people-hooks-test` OK, `wildlife-test` OK, `tree-people-probe` OK, `heightat-bench --repeat 25` (relative gate) OK, `areas-test` / `import-lint` / `runtime-only-lint` OK. **Strip unchanged** (`take.sh strip-light north-light checks` + `compare.mjs` against `m6-baselines` and against the T5 take): `layout-golden --diff` identical (strip and North), `npm run check` identical in all 25 strip modules, the streets / city / environment signatures identical (+0); against the T5 take the North differs in `landmarksTauride` (the numbers above) and `environment` (-20 trees: the parterre), the North streets signature identical. The strip never reaches `treeCleared` (its area module exports `HOOKS = {}`) nor a tiers row with the new keys.

**Open for the GPU QA and the lead**
* The lead confirms or rejects the parterre clearing (`PALACE_PARTERRE`): the alternative is a garden-owned planting of the palace side (T6 / T7), the fallback a T11 pose that does not claim the facade.
* GPU QA: the larch at 12 / 25 / 40 m (W-ground-12m, SE-flight-20m, N-ground-24m, S-walk of the round-1 script): a slender conifer on a visible stem, a greyer light green; the T11 frame (the facade across the lake, trees of the wood to both sides); the LOD2 stub of the larch (3.6 m, under one pixel at 150 m+).

### T5, fix round 2, 2026-10-23: the mapped OSM oaks obey the keep-outs (the nudge), one node refused (branch `tb-t5`; CPU only, no GPU)

**The findings** (the QA and the QA gate, both real and measured): the OSM oak at (1313.4, -1530.4) stood with its trunk ON the asphalt of the perimeter alley `w425201986` (its axis 1.17 m away, the ribbon 4.5 m wide), and the oaks at (1309.2, -1537.1) and (1289.7, -1553) cut the ribbon's edge (2.30 / 2.52 m from the axis, the trunk r 0.61 m). Cause: `planVegetation` planted the 7 `OSM_TREES` as **forced** (every rule but the water skipped), while every tree it placed itself keeps the way rule (half width + the 0.28 m ribbon pad + `KEEP_OUT.way` 1.5 m + the trunk radius). Measured before the fix (`osm-probe`, scratchpad): 4 of the 7 nodes break the rule (the three above and the SW oak (997, -1413.1) by 0.30 m: 1.2 m clear of the ribbon itself), and a single push off the nearest way does not help the first one: it lands 0.82 m from the axis of the 1.4 m trail `w112881682` that runs beside the alley there.

**What was done** (`vegetation.js`, `data/vegetation.js`, `vegetation-test.mjs`; no engine file): `reject()` lost its `forced` argument, **every zone answers every rule**. `plant()` takes `opts.nudge` (m): a position that a rule other than the water refuses moves to the **nearest clear spot** within that radius, rings of 0.25 m with 24 directions each (15 deg), the first clear candidate of the nearest ring wins (deterministic); none within the radius = refused with the node's own reason. `ZONES.osm.nudge` **4 m** (the mapping error of a park node; the relief's own 4 m disc round each node). The tree records its move: `tree.node = { x, z, d, why }` (null when it stands where it was authored), `stats.nudged[zone]` lists the moves, `stats.zones.osm.nudged` counts them. Result: **6 of the 7 oaks planted: 2 at their node, 4 nudged** (997, -1413.1) 0.5 m, (1308.9, -1547.5) 0.75 m, (1289.7, -1553) 2.25 m, (1309.2, -1537.1) 2.5 m; **the node (1313.4, -1530.4) is refused** (`osm:way` 1): it lies 1.17 m off the alley's axis and 4.02 m off the trail's, no spot within 4 m clears both rules, the first clear spot lies 6 m away across the alley at (1309.2, -1526.2). The solitary oaks of the meadows, which went through `plant()` with `forced: false` already, are untouched (5 of 6, the same positions). The gate's measure now holds for every tree: **no trunk within the way rule of any ribbon, no tree in the water or in a footprint.**

**Tests** (`vegetation-test` **92 checks**, +3): the OSM check measures with the test's own way geometry: every planted oak stands at a node or within `nudge` m of one, a moved oak's node fails the way rule and its new place passes it, the moved distance is the recorded one, the refused node has no candidate of the planter's ring pattern that clears the way rule; the numbers (6 / 2 / 4 / 1) and the stats; the mutant `ZONES.osm.nudge` 0 plants 2 oaks (the 4 nudged nodes refused too); the knob `nudge` 6 plants all 7 (the 7th 6 m across the alley), recorded, not shipped. Section 2 measures EVERY tree now (`notForced` = the whole list; the `forced` field is gone), as does the packing check.

**Numbers** (CPU): `landmarksTauride` (`npm run check:north`) **695,581 triangles** (fix round 1: 695,603; -22 = the refused oak at LOD3 and its blob), 134 meshes, 62 instanced meshes, 2,831 instances (-2), **2,605 colliders** (-1), BUDGET 765 k; the planting **1,011 instances at medium** (952 trees + 59 shrubs; the OSM zone 6, meadow 5, belt 118, avenue 488, shore 60, grove 334), 886 blobs; the LOD buckets 20,100 triangles at the HERO, 247,693 in the middle of the garden, 208,115 at the south gate, draws 11 / 50; the litter 16.5 % of the garden. The environment is identical to fix round 1 (`osm:garden` 7: the hook refuses the mapped nodes by the outline, not by position).

**Oracles** (cwd = the worktree, `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8000`; logs in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T5/fix2/logs/`, the take in `.../fix2/take/`): `vegetation-test` **92** OK, `north-hooks-test` **208** OK, `north-keepout-test --parallel --footprints` **18** OK, `check:north` OK, `views-check` OK, `tauride-cams-check --colliders` OK, `bridge-test --ducks` 48 OK, `pond-test` 62 OK, `ground-test` 85 OK, `data-test` OK, `tauride-height-test --model north --build landmarksTauride --assert-cars --no-selfcheck` OK, `north-height-test` OK, `north-people-hooks-test` OK, `wildlife-test` OK, `tree-people-probe` OK, `heightat-bench --repeat 25` OK, `areas-test` / `import-lint` / `runtime-only-lint` OK. **Strip unchanged** (`take.sh strip-light north-light checks` + `compare.mjs` against `m6-baselines`: `layout-golden --diff` identical (strip and North), `npm run check` identical in all 25 strip modules, the streets / city / environment signatures identical (+0); the only CHANGED row is `chk-hooks` exit 1 -> 0, the foundation baseline's own failing run, the MISSING rows the heavy lanes not taken). Against the fix-round-1 take (`--only north-,chk-`): the North golden, the streets signature and the environment identical; `landmarksTauride` differs by the numbers above only. The strip never imports `landmarksTauride`.

**Open for the lead**
* **The 7th oak**: the node (1313.4, -1530.4) is refused because the garden's own way rule (1.5 m + the trunk off every ribbon) cannot be met between the perimeter alley and the trail beside it. `ZONES.osm.nudge: 6` keeps it 6 m across the alley (the test pins that this works); the alternative is to accept 6 mapped oaks. One number, the lead's call; shipped: 4 m, 6 oaks.

### T6, 2026-10-24: the cast-iron fence and gates, the furniture, the lanterns, the monuments, the fountain, the orangery, the play fittings (branch `tb-t6` from `feat/areas` 3bbdf8e; CPU only, no GPU frame; software-GL stand-in frames)

**Commits** (every message ends with the Co-Authored-By line): `3471854` the three builders (`fence.js`, `furniture.js`, `monuments.js`), `data/furniture-design.js`, the entry, the claims, the planting's post pass, the worn spots, `furniture-test`; `a298fe6` the software-GL preview, the people probe, the pitch markings, the apron / plaza lift, the pose clearance of the lanterns, the knock census check 2b, the collider pins of the T3 / T4 tests, BUDGET; and the docs commit that holds this entry (`docs/AREAS.md` 8.15 / 10.7, `tools/tauride/README.md`).

**Decisions recorded as the lead stated them** (the T6 brief): (1) the palace parterre clearing (`PALACE_PARTERRE`, T5 fix round 1) is ACCEPTED (the garden front of the palace faced the lake across a lawn); (2) the fence stands on the street side of the T4a quay, the T4a colliders there stay; (3) the stretches in `FENCE_GAPS` stay open, they are real.

**What was done**
1. **The fence** (`landmarksTauride/fence.js`, 3 draws). `planFence` (pure, 12 ms) cuts every segment of the 14 mapped fence ways into `ceil(len / 2.7)` equal panels, the rule of the old `garden.js`: **932 panels** (the data's `FENCE_STATS.panels` 897 is `ceil(way length / 2.7)` per WAY; the per-segment count is what the openings of the data were derived from, and every one of the 13 opening clusters matches its panels to the millimetre: 3/3, 1/1, 4/4, 4/4, 2/2, 2/2, 4/4, 3/3 and five 3/3 on the pitch). **892 closed** (809 iron + 83 pitch), **38 open** (the 13 openings), **2 dropped** (the stub w112853216 reaches 1.7 m into the water: not built). The 4 `FENCE_GAPS` (141 / 50 / 15 / 15 m) have no panel. The table (panels / closed / open / piers): w10903027 (the south, east and west street fences, 1,198 m) 452 / 441 / 11 / 97; w426502809 (the Shpalernaya side) 118 / 114 / 4 / 28; w418563880 (the lake's north shore, 275 m) 109 / 109 / 0 / 28, 68 panels moved 0.18 m outward; w81524430 (the pitch ring, 260 m) 98 / 83 / 15 / 88 posts; w418967228 37 / 33 / 4 / 7; w418967227 30 / 30 / 0 / 7; w1458068098 26 / 26 / 0 / 8; w418563881 19 / 19 / 0 / 5; w420650657 15 / 15 / 0 / 6 and w420650658 12 / 12 / 0 / 7 (the two semicircles of the central round, claimed by the same ids); w83151420 10 / 6 / 4 / 2; w1293521581 3 / 3 / 0 / 3; w1458068097 1 / 1 / 0 / 2; w112853216 2 / 0 / 0 / 0. A PANEL is a granite plinth (0.3 m wide, -0.3 .. +0.22 m: it reaches the floor where the floor lies below the street) and ONE alpha-cut picket plane (9 bars with spears, two rails, a bulb under the top rail, a ring on every third bar: the strip's picket pattern of `landmarksE` / `landmarksF`, drawn into a 512 x 512 atlas that also holds the opaque band of the plinth / pier colours and the diamond mesh of the pitch fence: ONE material with vertex colours), 8 triangles, one instance, scaled in length to the panel (2.5 .. 2.7 m); PIERS (granite, 0.5 x 0.5 x 2.2 m, 34 triangles, one instanced mesh): **200** every 3 .. 5 panels, at every corner above 15 degrees, at the end of a fence and on both sides of an opening; the 7 STONE GATES (n950150459 west, n828488069 south, n967683857 north, n4222941897, the entrance n8486544221 and the private service gate n11695724997 in its two openings): two granite piers of 0.9 x 0.9 x 3.1 m with a cornice and a capstone, the INNER FACE on the join so that the clear width is the opening's width to the millimetre (5.4 .. 10.8 m), the iron leaves standing open and folded 2.2 m into the garden (a picket quad each, no collider: the opening stays free), and a cast-iron lantern on each pier of the five garden gates (10 in all); the 5 PITCH GATES are plain posts of the green wire-mesh fence (3.5 m, diamond mesh as an atlas band, a top rail, posts at every panel boundary: 88). THE BASE is the street side: `y = max(0, gardenHeight)` (the relief is zero within 8 m of the outline; the floor lies at -0.2 .. -0.45 where the lake and the west pond meet the outline and the quay skirt of T4a stands). An item on the water's edge moves outward until it is clear of it (`quay()`: panels by 0.18 m, piers by 0.34 m): **68 built panels (69 counting the dropped stub) and their piers along the lake's north shore stand on the street side of the quay**, and no panel of the 892 has a wet footprint point (the test: 0 of 4,460). Colliders: 1,106 (809 iron + 83 pitch panels as thin boxes with `fence = true`, 200 piers, 14 gate piers), `min(0, y) - 0.5 .. y + 1.9` (the T2 rule). **15,744 triangles** (6,472 panels + 6,800 piers + 2,472 static gates / leaves / pitch fence) against the 30 k, **3 draws**. **[Fix round 1: the stone (the plinths, the 200 piers, the gate piers, the pitch posts and rails) is now ONE opaque static mesh on the outline layer (`tauride-fence-piers`, 13,940 triangles), the panel mesh holds only the alpha-cut pickets (2 triangles an instance), the alpha-cut static mesh holds only the leaves and the diamond mesh: the piers are no longer an InstancedMesh (instanced meshes 79 -> 78, instances 4,270 -> 4,070), still 3 draws and the same 15,744 triangles.]**
2. **The furniture** (`furniture.js`). `planFurniture` (pure, 150 ms): the 132 benches and 167 bins through `knockInstances`: vertex-coloured models of `streets/vb.js` (the strip's lamp and car builder): the bench 52 triangles (black cast ends and back posts, orange-brown slats: `props/furniture.js benchPark`; fix round 2: 72 before, the boxes lose the faces nobody sees), the bin 18 (the black cast-iron urn, hexagonal, no bottom cap; 24 before), one `InstancedMesh` per part with `ctx.knock` (ONE knock cell for the whole garden, `FURNITURE_CELL` 4000 m in `data/furniture-design.js`; 7 + 7 draws in the 384 m cells before) and one static mesh per part without it. THE RULES: a bench faces the nearest way ribbon (by the ribbon EDGE) within 6 m (112 of 132), else the water within 30 m (0), else the garden centre (20); an OSM node whose footprint (a bench's 8 points: 1.8 x 0.55 m; a bin's 8 points on its circle) is not 0.3 m clear of every ribbon (the drawn edge = the nominal half width + the +-8 % of paths-draped.js; the stairs 1.3 m), is in the water or in a footprint, moves to the NEAREST clear spot (rings of 0.15 m, 24 directions: the T5 nudge): **16 benches** (largest 1.65 m) and **52 bins** (largest 3.3 m), none left stuck; every y is `gardenHeight`; the colliders (box 1.8 x 0.55 x 0.9 m, cylinder r 0.3 m, 0.78 m) reach from `min(0, y) - 0.5`. The 132 benches are published into `ctx.services.landmarks.benches` (`{ x, z, rotY, len, y, where: 'garden' }`, the way `landmarksE` does) and `landmarksTauride.benchPoints / bins / lamps`: `people/nav.js addBench` gives **122 of 132** a seat and an access node (the other 10 stand where no nav node is within reach), walkers sit on them (the probe: 9 distinct sitters, seat position and ground height exact). **13,512 triangles** (benches 9,504 + bins 4,008) against the 15 k.
3. **The lanterns**: **31** cast-iron lanterns (a green-black pole of 4.2 m, a four-sided glass lantern with a pyramid roof; 104 + 8 triangles after fix round 2, 120 + 8 before) on the 15 lit footways (data/paths.js `lit`; the lit bridge w81524417 and the lit stairs w425635247 excepted; 8 of them carry one: w81524425 7, w86283674 11, w116709203 2, w420650669 6, w422538570 1, w425188843 1, w81524423 2, w112729984 1), every 27 m from 8 m on ALTERNATE sides, 0.6 m beyond the drawn ribbon edge, never on a stair, a deck (+1.5 m), within 4 m of a gate, 12 m of another lantern, 6 m of a camera pose (the first version put a pole in the middle of the T03 frame: found by the software-GL render), 6 m of a sharp corner (the draped ribbon cuts it), within the clearance of a bench / bin / pier, or on a slope over 0.2; dropped by the rules: 6 corners, 2 ribbons, 1 spacing, 2 poses. Plus **10 lanterns (half size) on the gate piers**. Night as the strip's lamps (`streets/lamps.js`): a `MeshBasicMaterial` glow whose colour follows `ctx.shared.uNight` (0.7 + 1.5 n), and ONE shared additive light pool (radius 4.6 m, on the highest ground within 3.2 m of the foot, hidden with a knocked lantern: an `extraParts` entry). A lantern's knock record has 3 parts (body, glow, pool). **Furniture draws (fix round 2): 4 mesh draws on the page build too** (bench, bin, lantern body, lantern glow) **plus the shared additive pool, which draws at night only; 14,544 triangles in all** (it was 18,842 in 14 draws on the page build).
4. **The monuments** (`monuments.js`, 10 draws, 10,881 triangles against the 40 k / 12). `planMonuments` (pure) and `buildMonuments`: (a) the PIONEERS-HEROES MEMORIAL n4186236011 on the plateau M (1.2 m): a tapering limestone stele (2.5 x 0.9 m at the foot, 1.9 x 0.7 m at the top, **4.8 m** with the base), a two-step granite base (4.2 x 2.4 and 3.3 x 1.7 m), a recessed relief panel with a bronze star and flame, a paved apron of 3 rings x 28 pavers (radius 4.6 m, draped, **7 cm above the plateau so that the alleys' ribbons (+3 cm, polygon offset -1.4) do not cover it**: the first version at 3.5 cm was covered), a granite kerb, and the limestone CHEEKS of the three descents w425635247 / w1458068086 / w1458068092: two blocks per tread (the treads are read from `paths-draped.js plan.stairs`) beside the 0.2 m kerbs, 0.4 m wide, 0.62 m above each tread (the stair geometry is untouched); (b) CHAIKOVSKY n2974761162: a civil bust of the shared figure kit (`lib/figures.js bust`, detail 0.6, a full beard, 2,225 triangles, scale 1.6) on a granite pedestal of 1.1 x 1.1 x 1.6 m; (c) YESENIN n4222941889: a standing figure of the kit (`human`: a coat, curly hair; detail 0.55, 5,120 triangles) on a low granite plinth (the real statue's pose is not known to this repository: a design value); (d) the FOUNTAIN "Boy with a duck" n950150577: a granite basin of 2.6 m (rim 0.5 m, 0.34 m wide), water 0.12 m under the rim, a pedestal with the bronze boy and his duck (VB spheres and rods), a thin spout and 8 droplets animated through `ctx.onUpdate` (the jet height follows the page time), a paved plaza of 6.2 m with a granite kerb (also 7 cm above the ground); (e) the ORANGERY: the mapped OSM building **w23002927** (55.8 x 16.8 m, 6.6 m, outside the outline in the north-west corner (965, -1799)) is CLAIMED by the module (the building claim `ids` of `tauride-garden`: `city` no longer draws the block, -142 triangles) and drawn as a glasshouse: a brick plinth (0.9 m) round it with a 2.6 m door gap in the south end, 12 iron posts per side (11 bays), rafters and a ridge beam (7.6 m), translucent glass walls / glazed gable roof / glazed gables (one transparent vertex-coloured material, opacity 0.34, no outline), ten palms inside; four wall colliders; (f) the PITCH (w81524430): the turf in 12 mown bands (two greens), 48 marking lines (the touchlines, the halfway line, the centre circle, two penalty boxes) and two goals (7.32 x 2.44 m) at the short ends; the 3 PLAYGROUNDS (w188083933, w69029505, w188083935): a rubber / sand surface polygon and 8 structures (slide with a tower, swing frame, sandpit, spring rocker) placed by polar rules inside the polygon (2 m inset); the FITNESS STATION (w423708263): 4 pull-up frames along the polygon's axis. * `tools/tauride/pond-people-probe.mjs` (T4b): red with the first fence: the dam's deck, which T4b extended 2.3 m to dry land, ends **0.8 m from a closed panel of w418563880** (no mapped opening: the way ends 2.2 m before the panel and the old rule opens panels within 2.1 m); the nearest nav node of the east end lies beyond the fence, so "a route from axis end to axis end" had no route. The probe now asks the route between the points 1.5 m inside the two axis ends (all six sites route over the water, the dam with 3 nodes, 2 of them over the water on the deck): OK. See the open questions: the lead may want a 14th opening at the dam.

**Decisions and why**
* The fence is built per SEGMENT, as the old `garden.js` (932 panels, not the 897 of the data): the openings of the data were derived with that layout, and a per-way layout would shift every opening by up to a panel.
* One atlas texture and one material for the plinth, the pickets, the leaves and the pitch mesh: three draws. The alternative (geometry bars, ~100 triangles a panel) was 4 times over the 30 k.
* The base on the STREET side with a skirt, and the outward move on the quay (`quay()`): a fence standing on the floor would sink 0.45 m into the lake's quay; a fence at the outline would stand half in the water.
* Gate piers with the inner face on the join: the opening's width is the clear width (the test measures it), not the width minus the pier.
* Gate leaves without colliders: they stand at the edge of the opening, folded back; a collider there would narrow the walkers' lane by 0.1 m and the nav would see an obstacle at the gate.
* Piers are not knockable (the strip's iron fences are not; the cannon fence of NorthFull is a bollard-and-chain pattern); the panels and piers are plain colliders; the lanterns on the gate piers are visuals.
* Benches and bins MOVE (nearest clear spot) rather than stand on a ribbon: the OSM nodes of this park lie 0 .. 3 m from the alley axes (16 + 52 of 299); the move is recorded (`node`).
* The orangery: the mapped building is claimed. That is the one building the garden group claims; drawing a glasshouse over an unclaimed block would double the geometry.
* The planting's furniture pass is a POST pass, so that "the planting changes only where a keep-out bites" is literally true (3 trees); a rule in `reject()` re-rolled the zones.
* Lantern light pools are one shared additive mesh (the strip's pattern), not a light per lantern.

**Open for the GPU QA, T7 and the lead**
* **GPU QA** (what the CPU cannot show; the software-GL frames are a stand-in without the environment, the outline pass, the bloom or the real sky): T01 (the south gate from inside: the piers, the open leaves, the lanterns, the fence on both sides; Digit0 from the street), T09 and the north gate from both sides, T03 (the memorial: the stele, the apron, the cheeks, the lake behind), T08 (the fountain at 10 m and 25 m, the spout), Chaikovsky and Yesenin at 8 m and 20 m (the figure kit's bronze in the daylight: is Yesenin's pose acceptable), the orangery from the north-west corner at 30 m, T07 (the pitch: markings, goals, the mesh fence: the diamond mesh is an alpha-cut atlas band and may alias or vanish beyond ~60 m), a playground at 10 m, the fence from the street at eye level along Kirochnaya / Potemkinskaya / Shpalernaya (are the pickets visible at 50 .. 200 m: the atlas has mipmaps, alphaTest 0.4; the plinth and the piers carry the line when they thin out), the fence on the lake's north shore from the water side, the T12 aerial and the S / E / N / W aerials at 250 m, NIGHT: the lanterns lit along the main alley from T01 and T10, the gate lanterns, the pools; frame cost at T01 / T03 / T08 (the T6 pieces add 3 + 5 + 10 draws and 45 k triangles).
* **The dam's east end meets the fence** (above): a dead end for walkers; open panel 27 of w418563880 as a 14th opening, or let the dam end where the way ends?
* The orangery has no photo spot (no way within 30 m), no entrance (the building is a closed glasshouse; the nav's door points of the generic building w23002927 are still published by `city/entrances`, 2.6 m door gap in the south end).
* 10 of the 132 garden benches did not attach to the nav (no node within reach of their access point): T7.
* The 4 `chain` pitch details and the leaves' pickets are one-sided alpha planes: the back of a leaf seen from the street reads thin.
* `wildlife/world.js` still does not read `landmarksTauride.treeList` (T5's open item, T7); the pigeons / sparrows at the south gate stay (the gate piers stand 2.7 m from the node).
* Bake: `assets/baked` was not touched; the bake is stale for the garden (M8).

### T6, fix round 1, 2026-10-24: the stone on the outline layer, the people oracle green for the right reason, the furniture gate stated (branch `tb-t6`; CPU only, no GPU)

Three findings of the reviewers (lens, qa, qa-gate). Commit: see `git log` (the message starts "T6 fix round 1").

1. **qa (major): the outlines of the trees and walkers behind a pier were drawn across its face (T01, Digit0). CONFIRMED and fixed.** Cause: the gate piers (the 14 granite piers with cornice and capstone, the headline of T01 and of the south gate from the street) were part of the ONE static alpha-cut mesh (`tauride-fence-gates`, the atlas material with `alphaTest`) that `ctx.noOutline` puts on the colour-only layer: the normal / depth pre-pass the outlines are read from never saw them, so the lines of everything behind them showed through. The plinths of the panels (the instanced `tauride-fence-panels`, also alpha-cut) and the pitch posts had the same cause; the 200 ordinary piers were already an opaque layer-0 InstancedMesh (they were right). The fix, in `fence.js` only: `fenceGeometry` builds TWO world-baked geometries: **stone** (the 809 plinths of 6 triangles, the 200 piers of 34, the 14 gate piers, the posts and rails of the pitch; 13,940 triangles; a plain vertex-colour toon material, layer 0, casts shadows, LOD 650 m like the rest) and **alpha** (only the iron leaves of the gates and the diamond mesh of the pitch, no-outline layer); the instanced panel mesh keeps only the picket quad (2 triangles an instance, no-outline, alpha-cut). Still **3 draws and 15,744 triangles** (pickets 1,618 + stone 13,940 + alpha 186), instanced meshes 79 -> 78, instances 4,270 -> 4,070 (the piers are baked), colliders, claims and the knock census (330) unchanged. `furniture-test` **65 checks** (two new: the stone is ONE opaque mesh on the outline layer = 6 x 809 + 34 x 200 + the gate piers + the pitch posts, each of the 14 gate piers has its shaft in it, the cut-out meshes stay on the no-outline layer, 3 draws; plus a mutant of the predicate on the old arrangement). The GPU QA should look at T01 and Digit0 again: the pier faces should be clean granite; the lines of the trees and pedestrians stop at the pier edge, while the pickets (alpha) still show them through the bars, which is right.
2. **lens (major): `people-probe` red at 0.503 m. CONFIRMED; the cause named in the T6 entry was WRONG; fixed in a few strip-neutral lines.** The probe failed twice, not once: its node check printed `nav nodes on a hill (> 0.3 m): 114, off the ground by more than 5 cm: 32 {"b":32}, largest error 1.100 m` (the 0.503 line is the one that was noted). (a) The 32 nodes are the access nodes of the photo spots ringed round the Pioneers memorial (T6 published them): `people/nav.js attach()` is called by the bench / bus-stop / photo-spot code AFTER the "every street-level node gets its own height" pass, with `PAVE` 0.12 m, so a spot on the 1.2 m plateau stood 1.1 m under the ground (a walker going to photograph walked down into the slope and up again). (b) The follower of a tour group (`sim.js stepFollower`) took the height of its LEADER (`groundY(a, l.y)`) instead of the ground under itself: on a 1:4.5 slope with the follower 2 .. 3 m behind and aside the error is 0.5 m (the T2 "easing lag" note was a guess; the old limit passed by the luck of the crowd). The fix: **`nav.reliefY(x, z)`** (new in `people/nav.js`: no `HEIGHT_PATCHES` -> null; on a service deck (a garden bridge) -> null; else `heightAt + 0.02` on a hill or on the bank of a garden pond, the branch `surfaceY` already has) used in `attach()` (the access nodes of benches, stops and photo spots get the relief height) and in `stepFollower` (`groundY(a, nav.reliefY(a.x, a.z) ?? <the old expression>)`). The strip has no height patch, so both are inert there. Result: `people-probe` **RESULT: OK**, 114 nodes on hills all within 5 cm (largest 0.000 m), in motion n 434 p50 0.041 p90 0.103 **max 0.245 m** (T6 as built 0.503; the clean `feat/areas` 0.273), standing max 0.023; the limit 0.5 is unchanged. The first version of `reliefY` forgot the decks: `pond-people-probe` caught it (walkers up to 1.9 m off a deck), the deck exclusion is the fix; now the deck error is max 0.302 and RESULT OK as before. Files outside the phase's list were touched (`src/world/people/nav.js` +4 lines, `src/world/people/sim.js` 1 line): the finding named the easing / the sim; the lead may want to look at them.
3. **qa-gate (blocker): "furniture <= 15 k triangles / 4 draws". NOT changed: it needs the lead's decision; the numbers are stated here.** Measured strictly on the page build (`ctx.knock`, which splits each part into 384 m cells; `furniture-test` prints it as an info line): benches + bins **13,512 triangles in 7 draws**, the lanterns (body, glow, the shared pool) **5,248 triangles + the pool in 7 draws**, all furniture **18,842 triangles in 14 draws** (5 draws without `ctx.knock`: bench, bin, lantern body, lantern glow, pool). The plan C6 line reads "furniture 15 k instanced / 4" and the T6 brief puts BENCHES AND BINS under it (item 1) and gives the lanterns their own line (item 5: "instanced, 1-2 draws"): read that way the gate holds on triangles (13,512 <= 15 k) and the lanterns are a separate budget (the test asserts them apart: <= 12 k, and 5 draws for the five parts as built without the knock split). Read strictly (everything in `furniture.js`), the garden is **3.8 k triangles over**, and **the draws cannot reach 4 whatever the models**: they are the knock cells of the strip's own pattern (`lib/knock-inst.js`, one InstancedMesh per part per 384 m cell; the strip's benches and lamps cost the same per cell), so a 4-draw furniture means a furniture that is not knockable, or one cell. What would meet the triangles: the bench model 72 -> 48 triangles (-3.2 k) and the lantern 120 -> 80 (-1.6 k); that costs the slat / post detail the software-GL frames showed and is NOT done. The lead decides: (a) accept the split reading (benches + bins against the 15 k, the lanterns apart), (b) accept 18.8 k / 14 draws as the garden's furniture, or (c) order the model cuts. `BUDGET` in `data/furniture-design.js` is unchanged.

**Oracles of this round** (cwd = the worktree, `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8000`; logs in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/T6/fix1/`: `logs/`, `logs2/`, the take in `take/`): `furniture-test` 65 OK, `vegetation-test` 97 OK, `ground-test` 85 OK, `pond-test` 62 OK, `bridge-test --ducks` 48 OK, `tauride-height-test --model north --build landmarksTauride --assert-cars --no-selfcheck` OK, `north-knock-test` OK, `knock-consumers-test` OK, `tauride-cams-check --colliders` OK, `views-check` OK, `claims-draw-audit` OK (1 warning as before), `import-lint`, `runtime-only-lint`, `hooks-syntax-check` OK; people: `people-probe` OK (above), `pond-people-probe` OK, `furniture-people-probe` OK (9 distinct sitters, 6 photographers, 0 off), `tree-people-probe` OK, `people-test --at tauride` OK, `people-test` (North) OK, `north-people-hooks-test` (+ `--scene`) OK. **Strip unchanged** (`take.sh strip-light north-light checks suite strip-heavy` against `m6-baselines`): `layout-golden --diff` identical (strip and North), `npm run check` identical in all 25 strip modules, the streets / city / environment signatures identical, `suite-*` identical (`landmark-tree-bounds-test` red in both), `chk-*` same (`chk-hooks` exit 1 -> 0 is the foundation baseline's own failing run). `strip-people` differs from the FOUNDATION baseline in 60 lines, but the clean `feat/areas` 3bbdf8e run (`strip-people-clean.txt`, run read-only in `D:/git2/n4spb`) equals this tree line for line except the build times: the baseline is older than the merges T1 .. T5, the strip is unchanged by this round. `landmarksTauride` (`check:north`): 741,060 triangles (unchanged), 163 meshes, 78 instanced meshes (-1), 4,070 instances (-200), 4,055 colliders (unchanged).

### T6 fix round 2 (the furniture gate "15 k triangles / 4 draws" met strictly)

Three findings of the reviewers (lens major, qa major, qa-gate blocker), all the same open decision of fix round 1 (item 3). CONFIRMED as stated (18,842 triangles, 14 draws on the page build); it was not the lead's call after all, because both halves can be met without a visible loss, so they were met rather than waived:

* **Draws 14 -> 4 (+ the night-only pool).** The 14 were the knock cells of lib/knock-inst.js (one InstancedMesh per part per 384 m cell: bench 3, bin 4, body 3, glow 3, + the pool); the fix-round-1 sentence "the draws cannot reach 4 whatever the models" was wrong: knockInstances takes a `cell` argument. The garden (benches and bins x 926 .. 1489, z -1730 .. -1281) lies inside one cell of 4000 m (`FURNITURE_CELL` in data/furniture-design.js, passed to the three calls; the 330 knock records and their colliders are unchanged, a record's parts are the same mesh + index pairs). The cost: one LOD sphere of ~360 m radius per part instead of seven of ~270 m (the benches draw from 220 m beyond the garden's edge, all inside the garden), 13 k triangles of benches and bins in one draw instead of cell by cell; the people / knock tests and the frame worst case are unaffected.
* **Triangles 18,842 -> 14,544.** The models lose the faces nobody can see, with the same pixels: the bench 72 -> 52 (the cast ends and back posts lose their bottom, the seat and the back slat lose the bottom and the two end faces that lie inside the cast ends / posts), the bin 24 -> 18 (no bottom cap), the lantern 120 -> 104 (the four ribs lose their top and bottom inside the cup and the roof). Helpers in furniture.js: `openBox(skip)` (a BoxGeometry whose faces 0..5 = +x -x +y -y +z -z are dropped from the index), `binGeo`. The model cuts the LOG of fix round 1 listed (bench 72 -> 48, lantern 120 -> 80, "costs the slat / post detail") were NOT needed.
* **The gate is now asserted strictly:** `BUDGET.furniture` = { triangles 15,000 (all of furniture.js: benches + bins 9,870, lanterns 4,592, pools 82), draws 4 (the mesh draws), drawsNight 5 }; `furniture-test` checks it on BOTH builds (without `ctx.knock` in section 4, with the real core/knock.js in section 5) and that the whole furniture lies in ONE knock cell. The 5th draw is the shared additive light pool (the strip's lamps have the same), which is hidden by day (`visible = night > 0`): by day the furniture is 4 draws, at night 5. If the lead counts the pool, the literal gate is 1 over at night only.
* Oracles: `furniture-test` 67 OK, `npm run check:north` OK (landmarksTauride 736,762 triangles, was 741,060; 154 meshes, was 163; 69 instanced, was 79; colliders 4,055 unchanged), `north-knock-test` 7 OK, `knock-consumers-test` OK, `golden` and `golden:north` identical. `knock-stands-test` (strip) fails at HEAD of tb-t6 BEFORE this change too (carts 16, expected 17: the test was fixed in the later merge m7-polish on feat/areas, "knock-stands-test fixed"), unrelated.

### T7, fix round 1, 2026-10-24: the crows on the garden's crowns stand ON the drawn crown (branch `tb-t7`; CPU only, no GPU)

* QA major (the 198 crown perches sat 2.0 m inside the leaves; `crow-tree-side`, `crow-tree-top-from-above`): CONFIRMED. The perch `y = t.y + t.h - 0.6` used the nominal family height; the drawn crown differs from it three ways: the instance is stretched by `sy` (up to 1.25 x the scale), the five LODs of the canopy have different tops at the trunk axis (lime 13.06 / 14.22 / 13.56 m at scale 1 for LOD0 / 1 / 2; ash 11.5 / 14.7 / 14.8; elm 10.6 / 16.2 / 15.8), and the QA crows at 148 m stood in the LOD1 band (42 .. 150 m), whose top was 1.45 m above `t.h` (probe: lime `y 0, h 16.38, sy 1.253`: perch 15.78, tops 16.37 / 17.83 / 17.00 for LOD0 / 1 / 2 = the QA's 17.83; second tree 12.26 vs 13.08 / 14.25 / 13.58 = the QA's 14.25).
* Fix: `vegetation.js` publishes on every tree row of `treeList` `crown = [LOD0, 1, 2, 3]` (the highest of five rays dropped within 0.3 m of the trunk axis on the family's REAL canopy geometry, x `sy`, above the terrain under the trunk; one raycast per family and level) and `lod` (the level on the screen now: `assign()` keeps it). `wildlife/world.js` gives a perch of such a tree a `y` getter: `t.y + crown[min(lod, 2)] + 0.05`, evaluated when a crow lands (the level it lands on is the level that is drawn). The environment's trees and the strip are untouched (no `crown` = the old formula; the strip `wildlife-test` is identical).
* Checks: `tools/tauride/vegetation-test.mjs` 99 (+2: every tree row has `crown` x4 and `lod`, the LOD1 top of a lime equals the geometry hit); `tools/wildlife-test.mjs` (North) section 9 now asserts the perch equals the drawn crown at the current level and follows the level (LOD0 and LOD1 differ by > 0.3 m). Not seen: the GPU frames (the QA re-shoots `crow-tree-side` / `crow-tree-top-from-above`). Known limit: a crow that landed at > 42 m and then sees the player come inside 42 m keeps its LOD1 height until it flies (a crow scares at about 10 m; for ash / elm the LOD0 crown is up to 3 m lower).

### T7, fix round 2, 2026-10-24: a crow follows its crown when the level of detail pops (branch `tb-t7`; CPU only, no GPU)

* Lens major (the round-1 fix is right only at the level the crow landed at; the QA's own repro, a crow landed at 148 m = LOD1 and viewed at 12 m = LOD0, floated 1.5 m above the crown; a crow landed at LOD0 sank 1 .. 4 m once the camera left 46 m): CONFIRMED by reading the code (`setPerch` copied `b.y = p.y` once and `posePerch` drew `b.y`; the `y` getter of the perch follows `tree.lod` but nothing re-read it) and by the new probe below. Also the round-1 note was wrong: a tree crow scares at `perchScare 7 x 0.8 = 5.6 m` (not about 10 m) and the elm LOD0 / LOD1 difference is 4.7 m (not 3 m); it is the usual case, not a corner case.
* Fix (`wildlife/birds.js`, `updPerch`, one line): a bird perched on a tree perch whose tree publishes `crown` re-reads `b.y = b.perch.y` every frame (the getter is allocation-free), so the crow rides the LOD pop with the crown. Strip and the other trees (no `crown`) are untouched (`wildlife-test` strip: OK).
* Check: `tools/wildlife-test.mjs` (North) section 9, new probe: a crow set on a crown perch at 100 m (LOD1), the camera to 20 m (LOD0) and back to 100 m (LOD1), the tree level driven by the vegetation module's own `assign`; the crow y equals the drawn crown at each level (+0.05 m) and LOD0 and LOD1 differ by more than 0.3 m. Mutant (the `updPerch` line removed): the crow keeps its LOD1 y at LOD0 and the probe fails. `tools/tauride/vegetation-test.mjs` 99 / 0 failed.
* Remaining small limit: a crow that is FLYING to a crown perch aims at the crown height of the level at its launch; at touchdown `setPerch` snaps it to the level on the screen then (a pop of the same size only when the level changed during that flight). The pose is not lerped.

### Review fix, 2026-10-24: the adversarial review of the merged T1-T7 result (branch `tb-review-fix` from `feat/areas` 1623090; CPU only, no GPU)

Five confirmed findings (four minor, one the verifier lowered from major) and the minor / leftover lists of the reviewers. Every fix below has a check that turns red without it.

* **`car-keepout-sim` was red on the merged tree (minor, test honesty).** Since the T6 fence the colliders along the south edge stop the cars before the keep-out hook is asked, so the two "the proof can fail" controls (B1 mutant: 1 breach in 110 runs, B3: 1 of 20 head-on drives) no longer breached; the garden was never open (B1 / B2 real: 0 breaches), the guard was blind. Fix: the colliders that `fence.js` creates are tagged while the modules build (a wrapper on `Physics._insert` that looks at the stack), and the two controls run in the same world with the fence colliders taken out (`withoutFence`); the real-handling checks keep the fence standing. A new check asserts the tagging found at least 100 colliders. Result: B1 mutant 42 of 110, B3 14 of 20, `RESULT: OK (12 checks)` (was FAIL 2 of 11). Not run at the T6 / T7 gates: neither entry re-ran it; `npm run` has no script for it, run it at every phase gate that touches the fence or the keep-out.
* **The stair on level ground drew a 0.30 m pad (minor).** `paths-draped.js`: a stair whose ends differ by less than 0.1 m of terrain (w1458068093, the tag's 2 treads) got `n x riser` of rise and treads at 0.20 / 0.35 m on flat ground, which the Player (who follows the terrain) walks through. Now `level`: the treads stay at the terrain (`max(hLow, mesh + 0.01)`, no rise chain), 0 .. 0.10 m over the walking ground; `stairInfo` carries `level`. `ground-test` section 4: the riser check allows `riser === 0` for a level stair, and a new check pins the treads of the level stair to at most 12 cm over the mesh (the old build gave 0.20 .. 0.35; the check is red on the old file, verified). The other five stairs are unchanged: they rise with the terrain, their treads stand 0.01 .. 0.43 m over the walking ground (the partial overlap the finding measured), the bridge-head stairs are carried by the deck service. **Open (accepted limit, now written down): the treads of the stairs have no walk boxes, a walker's feet sink into them by up to 0.43 m (w1458068092); `Physics.addStairs` per stair would fix it, it changes the colliders of the module and needs a rebake.**
* **Picking the garden (Digit5) from the Places list while driving did nothing but toasted "Таврический сад" (minor).** `drive.place()` answers false when every free spot within 40 m is closed (the whole 81-point search is inside the keep-out); `goToView` dropped the answer. Now a refused spot shows "Сюда на машине нельзя: выйдите из неё (E) · Cars cannot go there: get out first (E)" and no place label, no area name, no gate hop (`src/main.js`). `north-hooks-test` static section: a text check that `goToView` keeps the answer (it was red while the regex was wrong, and reads the function body). R while driving is unchanged (the hero pose is outside the garden).
* **First-draw shader hitches (2 - 4 frames of 80 - 130 ms per program the first time it is seen) - NOT fixed.** Engine-wide (the strip has them too), `compileAsync` only links, `onFirstUse` -> `getProgramInfoLog` waits for the driver on the first draw. A warm-up that draws every material variant needs a GPU session to be checked (frame times, the order of programs); no change without it. Suggested: draw every unique material once in the load-time upload frame (`src/main.js` near the warm-up), starting with `lmOkt-bark-birch`, the three `drive-*` programs and the instanced toon / glass variants.
* **Furniture.** (a) A bench or a bin no longer stands in the keep-out of a bridge deck (`planFurniture`: `offDecks`, `k.hw + 0.1`; the bench n13371954335 stood 1.77 m from the dam axis end, inside its collider), and a bin never stands in a bench seat (`extra` of `place`: the bin's circle against the bench rectangle; two benches carried a bin, one of them 0.9 m from the T11 camera). (b) `furniture-test` 73 checks (+1): ABSOLUTE margins that do not come from the design datum (a bench footprint point 0.25 m, a bin centre 0.45 m off a ribbon edge), nothing on a deck, no bin in a seat. Verified red on the old `furniture.js` (1 on a deck, 6 bins in a seat) and with `BIN.edge: -1.0` (46 bins near a ribbon; the datum-based check stayed green, which was the finding).
* **`claims-draw-audit --strict`**: warnings (a claimed point / way / lawn that its owner draws nothing in) fail. `npm run claims-draw:north` runs it (the North has 0 warnings; the strip has 10, the castle's footways, so `claims-draw` for both areas stays as it was). Verified: `w31367323` added to `GARDEN_HEDGES` gives `STRICT: 1 warnings count as failures`, exit 1.
* **The gate machine allocated ~750 bytes per frame on the strip (minor).** `core/gates.js` `update()` made two spreads of the 13-key config, a `gateCoords` object per gate and three `Math.hypot`. The per-gate geometry is computed once at creation, the loop is plain arithmetic, the speed uses `Math.sqrt` (the geometry at creation still uses `Math.hypot`, so `d` is bit-identical). `gates-test` keeps the OLD implementation as an oracle and compares chips and offers over 60,000 random steps in walk / fly / car mode with declines and jumps (identical; depth within 1e-9), and a text check that the loop has no spread, no `Math.hypot`, no `gateCoords`. `core/gates.js` is a RUNTIME_ONLY file: the bake revision does not move for it.
* **The palace garden front from T11 (leftover 4)** (`keep-out.js` `PALACE_PARTERRE` `[1356, 1404, -1660, -1606]` -> `[1356, 1436, -1660, -1598]`). A CPU sight test from the T11 pose to the whole front (x 1347 .. 1420 every 3 m, 4 and 8 m high, the crowns of the environment's 6,523 trees and the garden's 985 as cylinders of 0.8 r from 0.3 h): the central part is clear (the T5 clearing holds, the garden itself has no tree in the 40 degrees of the view), the EAST WING (x 1394 .. 1420) stood behind 17 crowns of the wood (lime / maple / ash / birch / poplar, 70 .. 90 m from the pose, x 1406 .. 1433, z -1640 .. -1602). The box now reaches 32 m further east and 8 m further north: the environment plants 28 fewer trees (`check:north` environment: -28 instances, -56 triangles, -28 colliders, everything else identical). `vegetation-test` 100 checks (+1: no crown screens any part of the front, 0 now, 17+ with the old box; the probes `tc(1445, -1630) === false`, `tc(1380, -1590) === false`, the spare-width check allows 3 .. 45 m east of the bow); `north-hooks-test` and `north-keepout-test` carry the new box. **GPU look owed:** the east wing of the palace should now show from T11; the wood keeps its trees outside the box.
* **Documentation.** AREAS.md 5.3 said the engine job does NOT gate the deploy (the workflow and 11.3 say it does); rewritten. **A strip-visible change that the docs never named (listed so that it is on record):** `drive.js` `holdInput` holds the car for EVERY open modal panel (`ctx.ui.modalOpen`), not only the gate question; on the strip the touch Menu and Controls panels (`core/touch.js`) are modal, so a moving car now gets full brake (brk 1 above 0.8 m/s), a car at rest the handbrake, where the old code gave brk 0.4 (which accelerates a stopped car backwards). Intended and better; not reverted.
* **Not fixed, with the reason (nothing here is a gate failure).**
  * The play fittings (8 structures of the 3 playgrounds), the fitness bars and the pitch goals have no collider (walkers pass through): decision recorded, they are low and open; add small boxes for slides / swings / rockers if a collision is wanted (`monuments.js`, colliders and a rebake).

  * A litter bin inside the seat of two benches: FIXED above; 12 more benches have a bin within 1.4 m outside the seat (left).
  * Pedestrians stuck at the north end of the Kirochnaya crossing 20 m west of the south gate, and the walk along the gate axis x 1226 that stops at z -1261.9: both are the same kind of thing, a parked car (box 1.92 x 4.78 at (1227.1, -1263.2) and another at (1209.3, -1263.7)) in the street in front of the main gate. `HOOKS.parkingReject` covers Paradny only. A parking keep-out round the gates is a change of the hook contract (`north-keepout-test` pins the rule as exactly the podium) and of the baked streets; proposed, not done. `people-test` does not grade the stuck list (the line is printed).
  * The ghost hairline at (1100.6, -1597), the stale-bake card wording (`core/boot-guard.js`), the pond water colour (GPU look), the frustum culling of the garden trees (`frustumCulled = false`, 80 % of the tree triangles submitted outside the view, phones): left; the first two need a GPU session, the last a per-sector split of the instanced meshes.

### T8b, 2026-10-24: the garden nursery, a user fix (branch `tb-t8b` from `feat/areas` e019172; CPU only, no GPU frame; software-GL stand-in frames)

The user (project owner) asked: "update area on 1041.9,40.7,-1670.1,14.5,0.5 - should look like the real air photo" (`D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/user/user-lot-real-airphoto.png`, taken from about 1180.2, 118.7, -1667.6 looking WEST; our screenshots `user-lot-ours-*.png`). The lot is the old nursery of the garden north-west of it, between Potemkinskaya (west), Shpalernaya (north) and the garden fence (east). **What our scene showed:** the generic yard ground (mottled grey asphalt, moss, brick) and three 4-storey yellow blocks. **Commits** (every message ends with the Co-Authored-By line): the nursery module, its claims, the budget and the North golden; the hooks and the tests; the docs and the polish.

**What was done**
1. **Reading the photo against the data.** The photo looks west: its top is Potemkinskaya, its right edge Shpalernaya, the pond is at the bottom left. So the long glasshouses run EAST-WEST (not north-south), in two groups of three with one wide house between them, and two houses across the EAST end on each side of a concrete box (the photo's "south end"); the yard of the classical buildings is at the WEST end (the U-blocks `w23002928 / 36 / 41`, the pavilion `w259579860`: already in the scene, left alone), the T6 orangery (`w23002927`, x 951 .. 970) is the long glass house at the photo's top right, and the long strip at its right edge is `w30324513` (OSM kind=greenhouse, 121 x 15 m, no height). The two yellow blocks of the user's screenshot are `w1457964048 / w1457964049` (18 x 17 m, no `height`, no `levels`: the city guessed 4 storeys). The Yandex satellite shows the two lawns that came after the nursery (x 997 .. 1078, z -1772 .. -1620): the houses stand on them. The OSM buildings overlaid on the satellite with the user's mapping align (plots in `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/t8b/`).
2. **The ground** (`landmarksTauride/nursery.js`, `data/nursery.js`). ONE flat mesh over the lot ring (22 vertices, **35,352 m2**, y = 0.005, polygon offset 2: the garden floor's own numbers) with its own toon material (the T3 pattern: `onBeforeCompile`, one category per pixel from the garden's noise tile: brown earth, sand patches, pebbles, dry-grass lanes, a pale-gravel fenced yard, the dark soil under the glass with weeds, gravel tracks) driven by a 1 m RGBA8 mask built here (`planNurseryMask`: 189 x 234 cells, 176,904 bytes: R tracks 4,387 cells, G soil 5,047, B yard 5,424, A dry grass 7,281). The environment ground is cut out under it: `HOOKS.groundHoles` (`hooks/ground.js`) now hands out TWO rings, the garden outline and the lot ring, each moved in by 6 cm. **The lot ring shares the garden outline's west edge EXACTLY** (the garden vertices 56 .. 60 are consecutive in the ring): the two floors meet with no gap and no overlap; the lot and the garden polygon share no area (35,321 lattice points of 1 m in the lot, 0 in the garden). The ring follows the fences and walls of the data (w1457964050 south, w259579861 / w418967229 / w418967230 west, w1164451899 / 900 north-west, the footway w112880646 and the long building's north face north); the sidewalks and footways of `streets` lie above the floor as above the environment ground (a scan of every module at ground level in the lot: only `pt-*` ribbons of the yard's footways and the service lane at x 976, 8 `city-fine` triangles, the nursery's own meshes and the 28 + 28 ground triangles of the 6 cm band).
3. **The glasshouses.** Nine: S1, S2, S3 (the south group), M (the wide one, 12 m), N1, N2, N3, and the two cross houses C1 / C2 on each side of the concrete box. 10 m wide (M 12), 46 .. 60 m long, gaps of 6.5 m inside a group (a row of young trees in each), 16 / 9 m between the groups (the cross tracks): DESIGN numbers read from the photo and put on the satellite's lawns (the photo is the reference). Each: a concrete plinth 0.45 m, a steel-rib frame (eave 2.5 m, ridge 4.2 m: a shallow gable), bay bars every 2 m (the posts and rafters, wide) and glazing bars every 1 m, **7,314 panes in all (830 per long house); a deterministic hash (house seed, side, row, column, modulated by a patch noise of 6 x 3 panes so that the intact ones come in groups) keeps 1,857 = 25.4 %** (21 .. 32 % per house). The frames are ONE double-sided opaque mesh on the no-outline layer (hundreds of thin bars would turn into black outlines), the glass ONE transparent double-sided mesh (opacity 0.42, pale blue-grey). The strips that cross each other lie in one plane: they stand on three layers 1.8 cm apart (`HOUSE.layer`) and the gable bars 2.5 cm out of the end plane, the hidden faces of the plinth and the parapets are left out, so that **no two opaque surfaces are within 1 cm** (the scan below). One box collider per house (4.6 m high, not enterable).
4. **The three buildings.** `groups.js` gets the sub-group `tauride-nursery` (`w1457964048`, `w1457964049`, `w30324513`; the literal equals `data/nursery.js CLAIMED_IDS`): `city` no longer draws them (-622 triangles, -14 colliders) and the module draws them low and plain: two concrete boxes (4.4 / 4.8 m, a flat roof with a parapet, a dark roofing patch, a stub, a window row, a door) and the annex (5.4 m walls, a grey gable roof, 33 windows a side, a plinth), each with an oriented-box collider. **Why not `known-data.js`** (the task's first suggestion): `src/areas/north/known-data.js` must equal `tools/strip-data-known.mjs --check` of the source (the generated cut; `area-data-test` runs it), a hand row makes it red. The answer to "check the tags": the data does NOT say 4 storeys, the city's default did; the claim redraws them.
5. **Trees, piles, crates.** 122 young trees (<= 150) in ONE instanced mesh (a 92-triangle template: a tapering trunk, branches, light buds; 5 .. 7.8 m; deterministic jitter): the lanes between the houses, both sides of the middle track, a row north of the last house and an orchard row; kept 2.35 m off the houses, 2 m off the buildings, off the tracks, piles and crates. Two earth piles (sand and dark) and two stacks of crates. The environment's own trees, grass tufts and props are kept out of the lot by the hooks: `treeReject` answers `'nursery'` (`hooks/keep-out.js`, `inNursery` of the pure data module: **0 environment trees in the ring now, 5 without the hook**), `tuftReject` and `propReject` cover it (strip-neutral: gated by the module list, the strip exports no `HOOKS`).
6. **Walkability.** Before: the lot was open yard. Now: the 9 houses and the 3 buildings are boxes (12 colliders: 4,016 -> 4,028); the tracks (12, 3 .. 4.4 m wide) and the 6.5 / 9 / 16 m lanes are open (a walker walks every track end to end and every lane between neighbouring houses: 0 colliders across); people use footway graphs and the lot has none besides the yard's own (unchanged).

**Decisions and why**
* **Nine houses, not seven:** the user wrote seven (3 + 1 + 3) and also "one long one across the south end on each side of a small concrete box"; the photo shows both (7 + 2). All nine are built; the test names them (7 along x, 2 along z).
* The floor is a flat polygon (`layout.heightAt` is 0 over the whole ring: 1,591 points) with its own small material rather than a second instance of the garden's `ground-material.js` (587 x 536 m mask, hills, shore, canopy, stripes): the lot has none of those; the same pattern with a lot-sized mask (177 kB) and no unused channels.
* The lot ring is NOT cut by the building footprints: the floor lies under the buildings (nothing sees it), so there is no hole to keep watertight.
* Buildings claimed through `groups.js`, not through `known-data.js` (above). The long annex is claimed although the user named two blocks: it is the biggest wrong thing in the lot (a 121 m yellow block with a red roof across the photo).
* The engine files are not edited at all: the hooks use the existing `groundHoles`, `treeReject`, `tuftReject`, `propReject` call sites; the strip exports no `HOOKS`.

**Numbers** (CPU; `node tools/area.mjs north tools/check.mjs`): the nursery adds **20,626 triangles in 5 draws** (floor 20, frames 5,150, glass 3,714, trees 11,224, buildings and details 518; budget 40 k / 6), `landmarksTauride` **738,046 -> 758,672**, BUDGET 815 k -> **835 k**, 154 -> 159 meshes, 12 more colliders; `environment` +186 triangles (the lot hole breaks merged ground blocks into cell pieces), -8 instances and -5 colliders (the trees of the lot); `city` -622 triangles, -14 colliders (the three buildings). `layout-golden` North regenerated: 63 leaves, all of them `GROUPS` / `SUBGROUPS` (the new sub-group) and the probe sets (the probe grid follows the group anchors: 503 -> 504 points); the strip golden identical.

**Strip unchanged** (`D:/git2/n4spb-work/m6-baselines/take.sh` + `compare.mjs` against a take of the clean branch before the first edit, `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/t8b-base` / `t8b-after`): `layout-golden` strip identical, `check` of the strip **25 modules identical**, the strip streets / city signatures and `environment-regress` identical; the `chk-*` lane (areas, import-lint, runtime-only-lint, gen-area-html, layout-test, landmarks-groups-check, views-check north, area-garage-check both, owns-table, north-hooks-test 210 -> 212) green; North `streets` signature identical; the strip environment snapshot (`take.sh snapshots`) identical. **The North environment snapshot against a clean `feat/areas` export (`git archive`, `D:/git2/n4spb-work/tauride/t8b-cleanout`): 43 changes, all inside the lot box + 8 m: 5 trees removed (`rej yard:nursery` 0 -> 5), 38 tuft cells thinner (the earth has no tufts), 0 props, 0 parked slots / lines / lanes / lamps changed** (`north-env-snapshot --diff ... --allow 930,1116,-1826,-1594`: RESULT: OK). `car-keepout-sim --quick` OK (12 checks: the keep-out hook of the cars is untouched, `keep-out.js` was edited). `people-test --at tauride --minutes 4` RESULT: OK (violations all 0, stuck walkers 5: the parked cars in front of the south gate, as on the merged tree).
